# Changelog

What each change added, newest first. A visible change has a clip: click the still to play it. `python3 tools/changelog/changelog.py page --open` plays them all in one page.

## 2026-11-27

- **699** The project index reads every folder once when the project root comes back after it could not be looked at, so a file whose held change was folded to a removal while the root was gone is listed and published again (as created, with the stamp it has now) even when its folder is older than the 2 s racy window and its time never moved *For you: If the project folder is moved away and back while a file in it is being written, that file shows up again in the Files tree and the finder instead of staying missing.* *No clip: an index fix measured headless; proved by tests/the_project_index_is_the_one_watcher.rs (a_file_dropped_while_the_root_was_gone_is_listed_again_when_the_root_comes_back: fails 3 of 3 without the fix, the listing lacking the file, and passes with it)*
- **644** tests: two project-index tests no longer fail on a loaded host. The held-change test moves the root the moment the index says it holds a change (`Follow::held`, a count the index keeps of what it holds), not after 80 frames that a loaded host stretched past `MAX_HOLD`, so the change was published as it should be and the test failed every full-suite gate on big; the long save paces its five writes on the wall clock, not on frames that outlasted the 100 ms debounce *No clip: tests and a test lever; no editor behaviour changed; proved by tests/the_project_index_is_the_one_watcher.rs (a_change_held_when_the_root_goes_does_not_end_the_refusal and a_save_that_takes_longer_than_the_debounce_is_still_one_event, each 10 module runs on bmb beside a -j 28 loop of the index tests and seven `yes`; the held test still fails 5 of 5 when `publish` clears the failure again)*
- **541** tests: raising the terminal with one open shell lays its words out on the next frame and spawns no text, counted in frames and nodes rather than timed; the switch drive chooses a plain shell in its own home so its 20 raises have words to wait for *No clip: tests and tooling only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by tests/a_raised_terminal_shows_its_words_on_the_next_frame.rs (a_raise_lays_out_the_shells_words_on_the_next_frame_and_spawns_no_text)*
- **541** The Kit workspace's first scan of the project's meshes runs on a compute thread while the person is on another workspace, so the first arrival at Kit is answered from it and opens no mesh in its own frame (it was 149.6 ms of a 234 ms frame on the review's bedroom project) *For you: Switching to the Kit workspace for the first time in a session no longer pauses while the editor measures every mesh file; it has already looked at them in the background.* *No clip: work counted headless; proved by tests/a_workspace_switch_keeps_the_scene.rs (a_second_arrival_at_kit_opens_no_mesh_the_first_one_measured: the look on the Map opens each candidate once, the first arrival none)*
- **678** tools: the workspace-switch and terminal-raise drive is tools/demo/switch_drive.py, which starts the editor itself and writes a summary under docs/bench/switch/ that a release prints beside the benchmark (tools/release/bench_advisory.sh) *No clip: tooling only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by the drive's own run on big, whose summary is docs/bench/switch/2026-11-27-acceptance.json*
- **541** tests: a workspace switch's work is held to the budget measured for it, at most 700 new UI nodes and 280 texts per switch, rather than to a wall clock that is the host's as much as the editor's *No clip: tests only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by tests/a_switch_frame_names_its_systems_and_keeps_its_budget.rs (a_switch_spawns_no_more_than_its_measured_budget)*
- **678** The frame profile names the systems a frame spent its time in, not only the schedules: a workspace switch's frame lists the door's sweep and spawners (screen::close_the_door, chrome::spawn_frame and the rest) and bevy_ui's layout, heaviest first, in blueprint/state.frame.systems *For you: When a workspace switch is slow, the editor's frame report now says which of its own steps took the time, so the slow part can be found without guessing.* *No clip: a profile measured headless; proved by tests/a_switch_frame_names_its_systems_and_keeps_its_budget.rs (a_switch_frame_names_the_systems_in_the_doors_path)*
- **678** A workspace switch keeps the activity bar and the toolbar as it keeps the menu bar: both are lifted off the frame the switch sweeps and put back in the new one, so a second switch spawns 429–667 UI nodes where it spawned 561–763 (only the lit tab's rail moves under the activity bar); the toolbar is swept on a workspace without the map's tools *For you: Switching workspaces does a little less work, because the workspace tabs at the left and the tool buttons under the menus are no longer rebuilt on every switch; they look and behave as before.* *No clip: work counted headless; proved by tests/a_workspace_switch_keeps_the_menu_bar.rs (a_switch_keeps_the_activity_bar_and_spawns_none_of_its_nodes, a_switch_keeps_the_toolbar_where_the_workspace_has_it_and_spawns_none_of_its_nodes)*
- **700** The empty map's prompt (Place a piece from the library, Open library) stands only on the workspaces that show the map, Map, World, Kit and Animation; on Files, Design, Sound, Agent, Project and Game it is gone, where it was drawn over the Files editor and took the pointer at its centre (found by #540's capture test; the stills are the Files editor on an empty map after and before, both read back by that test) *For you: When a map has nothing on it yet, its prompt to place a piece now shows only over the map, so it no longer sits on top of the file editor or catches clicks and scrolling there.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-27-700-files-without-the-map-prompt.png"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-27-700-files-without-the-map-prompt.png" width="320" alt="after: the Files editor with nothing over it"></a> <a href="docs/loop/captures/540-2026-11-27/minimap-opened.png"><img src="docs/loop/captures/540-2026-11-27/minimap-opened.png" width="320" alt="before: the prompt over the Files editor"></a>
- **472** tests: the section-cut pixel test draws the room it samples: its ground and carve list are written through the model's own writer into the kit's directory, where the editor reads them (hand-written one level up, the carve list in a shape syntax the reader refuses, neither was read, so every picture held the map's wireframe and grid and no rock), it waits for the rock to be drawn, frames the room's centre under the picture's centre texel (it was left on the whole-map frame, 10.7 m under the room's middle, and the Map camera's axis is the docked viewport's centre, not the picture's) and stands the rock 2 → 22 m over the room (centred on the soil, it stood -8 → 12 m and the room was an open pit with no ceiling) *No clip: a test's setup, nothing a person sees changes; proved by tests/a_section_cut_shows_the_floor.rs (a_section_cut_draws_the_room_floor_and_the_rock_is_gone, without_the_plane_the_centre_is_the_ceiling_and_not_the_floor, the_plane_is_put_back_on_the_camera) under Xvfb on a real device*
- **540** tests: the minimap's band follows the view when a wheel turned over the editor scrolls it with no edit, and a frame of a file opened for reading, captured on a real device, paints the bar colour in the lane; the harness's real device now draws: it never finished its app, so the RenderDevice never arrived and every pixel test skipped on every host *No clip: tests and the test harness, nothing a person sees changes; proved by tests/the_minimap_is_laid_out_when_a_file_opens.rs (the_band_follows_the_view_when_the_wheel_scrolls_it_with_no_edit, a_captured_frame_of_a_file_opened_for_reading_paints_the_bar_colour_in_the_lane) and tests/a_section_cut_shows_the_floor.rs, which now runs where there is an adapter*
- **612** A wrapped line in the Files and Design editors hangs its later rows under its first word, so a Markdown list item, a quote or an indented line continues under its own text and not at the editor's left edge (the owner's pick of 2026-11-27; gooey's `CodeWrapIndent::Same` beside `CodeSoftWrap`, bevy_gooey#126; the stills are two captured frames of one page, with the hang and then without it as the editor drew it before, and tests/a_markdown_page_wraps_and_is_drawn_in_ink.rs reads the hang off both: every later row of the `- ` item at column 2 with it and at column 0 without it) *For you: In a Markdown page or a text file, a long list item, quote or indented line that wraps now continues under its first word instead of at the left edge.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-27-612-wrap-indent-on.png"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-27-612-wrap-indent-on.png" width="320" alt="after: the item's later rows under its text"></a> <a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-27-612-wrap-indent-off.png"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-27-612-wrap-indent-off.png" width="320" alt="before: every row at the edge"></a>
- **612** The Files editor's own stop for the find bar's keys is gone: gooey's bar keeps them itself since v0.64.2 (bevy_gooey#128), so `Enter` in the query still steps to the next match and leaves the page as it was *No clip: an editor workaround deleted, nothing a person sees changes; proved by tests/the_file_editor_finds_with_a_count_and_wraps.rs (Enter x4 reads 1 of 3, 2 of 3, 3 of 3, 1 of 3 with the page unchanged, with `files::keep_the_find_bars_keys` deleted)*
- **552** The place list's rows stand their cells at the top with gooey's `RowLayout { align: FlexStart }` on the row, which gooey writes on a list row since v0.66.0 (bevy_gooey#127), instead of a `TextFlow` align on each cell *No clip: a layout written another way, the same rows; proved by tests/the_place_list_says_what_arms_a_row_and_lines_its_cells_up.rs (the cells' first lines line up and a long name grows only its own row) and tests/the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted.rs*
- **540** tests: the minimap's view band is held to the view on the frame a file opens and after a scroll with no edit: it starts at the lane's row of the view's first line and is as tall as the lines in view, which gooey's band fix in v0.64.1 (bevy_gooey#123) made true *No clip: tests only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by tests/the_minimap_is_laid_out_when_a_file_opens.rs (a_file_opened_for_reading_draws_its_minimap_bars_on_the_first_frame, the_band_follows_the_view_when_it_scrolls_with_no_edit)*
- **698** A Rust file open in Files completes and hovers from rust-analyzer, tells it every save so its check runs again, and sends each change as the one edit that makes it; `docs/design/rust-server.md` says what the server adds and what the tokenizer keeps *For you: In a Rust file of a Cargo project, typing a name or pressing Ctrl+Space lists rust-analyzer's completions at the caret and Enter writes the chosen one, resting the pointer on code shows what rust-analyzer says about it, and a save makes it check the project again so the errors follow the file.* *No clip: a fixed server's answers read back from the editor headless; proved by tests/a_rust_files_completion_and_hover_come_from_its_server.rs (a typed letter lists the stub's two writable items at the caret in its order and Enter writes its edit, the hover note is its text over its range, edits reach it as edits and one save is told) and files_lsp's unit tests (positions both ways, the one edit between two texts, which completion items the list keeps)*
- **678** A workspace switch keeps the menu bar instead of building it again: the bar is lifted off the frame the switch sweeps and put back on top of the new one, so a second switch spawns 503–699 UI nodes where it spawned 1,416–1,618 *For you: Switching workspaces does less work, because the menus at the top of the window are no longer rebuilt on every switch; they read and behave as before.* *No clip: work counted headless; proved by tests/a_workspace_switch_keeps_the_menu_bar.rs (a_switch_keeps_the_menu_bar_and_spawns_less_than_the_bar_holds)*
- **663** bevy_fairy is pinned at `c82f594` (0.13.0 plus #18 and #19), so a `bevy_fairy/input` call that sends `modifiers` is refused by name instead of answered `success: true` with the bare key tapped, and the guide's steps are evaluated by fairy's own `GuidePlugin`, which the editor's shared plugin list now adds, so the first-map tour advances in the headless harness as in the binary *No clip: a dependency pin, one plugin in the shared list and doc lines; proved by bevy_fairy's tests/plugin_input_over_http.rs (dark-incorporated/bevy_fairy#18: at the old pin 06c9c18 the measured Ctrl+7 call tapped a bare Digit7 and answered success, 4 of 5 passed; at 5522058 all 5 pass), its tests/plugin_guide_steps.rs (dark-incorporated/bevy_fairy#19: GuidePlugin alone advances a step; the checkpoint is asked once a frame whoever added the guide) and tests/the_first_map_tour.rs (arming_and_placing_advance_it_to_step_three and turning_and_saving_finish_the_tour, which stayed on step one with the 0.13 pin and no GuidePlugin)*
- **536** The status band's two lights are drawn at the label rung of the type scale instead of the body's size, the data table's cells wrap at their column, and four layout tests' gooey exemptions are narrowed to what remains (cells 40/14/12 to 0/1/0, LED words 2 to none) *For you: The agent and decision-model lights at the foot of the window are a step smaller than before, the size of a label rather than of body text, and a long path or value in the Data panel's table breaks onto a second line inside its column instead of running past it.* *No clip: sizes and line breaks read headless; proved by tests/the_agent_and_the_decision_model_share_one_place.rs (the_band_lights_are_drawn_at_the_bands_label_rung), tests/a_panel_census_at_five_scales_over_populated_content.rs, tests/no_panel_draws_a_control_past_its_own_content_box.rs, tests/the_left_pane_reflows_when_the_divider_moves.rs and tests/the_chrome_bars_readouts_are_not_a_second_menu.rs (exemptions narrowed to one-line cells; the LED exemption removed)*
- **678** Arriving at Kit opens no mesh the last look already measured: a scan of a directory and a library nothing has touched since is answered from that look, so a Kit switch no longer re-measures every `.glb` the kit does not hold *For you: Switching to the Kit workspace on a project with many meshes is quicker: the meshes it already measured are read again only when they change on disk, and one you drop in while elsewhere still shows up when you arrive.* *No clip: work counted headless; proved by tests/a_workspace_switch_keeps_the_scene.rs (a_second_arrival_at_kit_opens_no_mesh_the_first_one_measured) and blueprint_model's import::tests::a_second_look_at_an_untouched_directory_opens_no_file*
- **692** The agent is the Night Shift, two words, spelled once: the Agent panel is titled `NIGHT SHIFT` and wraps between its words, and every history row, receipt, status note and setting builds its name from that one spelling *For you: The Agent panel's title now reads NIGHT SHIFT and, when the panel is narrow, breaks into NIGHT / SHIFT instead of NIGHTSHIF / T; the history, the look chip and the Follow the agent setting say "the Night Shift".* *No clip: a title's line breaks measured headless; proved by tests/no_panel_draws_a_control_past_its_own_content_box.rs (the_agent_panels_title_breaks_between_its_words at interface scales 1.0 and 1.6) and tests/the_history_scrolls_five_thousand_lines.rs (an_agent_line_says_the_night_shift_and_a_persons_says_you)*
- **669** The status band carries an `Art 32%` control whose label is the art's share of the window, and a click toggles Work and Look; `blueprint/state` reports the same number as `rects.art_share`, and `rects.window` is the largest root, so a full-width bar no longer reads as the window *For you: The bottom bar now says how much of the window your game's art has. Click it to hide the editor around the art (Look), and click it again (it stays in the corner) to get the editor back exactly as it was (Work).* *No clip: a layout measured headless; proved by tests/the_art_share_control_gives_the_art_the_window.rs (the label is the measured share and the state reports it; one click gives the art the window and a second gives Work's rects back exactly; run_command reaches the control; the window is the largest root and not a bar as wide as it)*
- **612** The Files editor names the section the caret stands in: a row over the page reads its name and every heading that holds the caret *For you: Above a Markdown page in the Files workspace a line now says where the caret is, such as notes.md › Combat › Weapons; it follows the caret, and a file without headings does not draw it.* *No clip: words and boxes measured headless; proved by tests/the_file_editor_names_the_section_the_caret_is_in.rs (the nested chain, the walk from section to section, drawn above the editor inside the page, gone over a map and back with the page)*
- **612** Enter in the file editor's find bar steps to the next match and no longer puts a line break in the page; the bar's count and its wrap are measured where a person reaches them *For you: In the Files workspace, Cmd+F (Ctrl+F on Linux) opens find; it says 1 of 3, Enter goes to the next match and round from the last to the first, and Previous goes back round, without changing the file.* *No clip: keys and words measured headless; proved by tests/the_file_editor_finds_with_a_count_and_wraps.rs (the chord opens the bar inside the editor with the keyboard in its query; Enter x4 reads 1 of 3, 2 of 3, 3 of 3, 1 of 3 with the page unchanged; Previous from the first is the last); the bar is gooey's, and gooey's own fix is dark-incorporated/bevy_gooey#128*
- **612** The file editor comments the lines under the caret on `Cmd+/` in the file's own syntax, and its caret-line band and bracket marks are measured where a person sees them (the owner's picks of 2026-11-27 were these three, the find bar's count, the wrapped-line indent and the breadcrumb; multi-cursor was not picked) *For you: In the Files and Design editors, `Cmd+/` (`Ctrl+/` elsewhere) comments the caret's line or the selected lines — `//` in Rust, `.ron` and Rhai, `#` in YAML, `<!-- -->` round a Markdown line — and a second press takes the comment off. `Cmd+Z` takes a press back like any edit. The lit caret line and the framed matching bracket were already drawn by the editor and stay as they were.* *No clip: behaviour measured headless; proved by tests/the_file_editor_lights_the_caret_line_marks_brackets_and_comments_on_cmd_slash.rs (the band on the caret's row and off the ground's colour; the two bracket marks on their rows ten columns apart; Cmd+/ on a .rs line adds `// ` and a second press restores the file; Cmd+Z after a third press restores the text and a second Cmd+Z takes back the press before it, the toggle being one undo step of the person's own through bevy_gooey#125's `CodeEditor::edit`; a selection comments every line with words; each language's syntax)*
- **541** A workspace switch decodes no cached palette portrait the session already read: every arrival rebuilt the palette's pictures by reading and decoding each one from `target/blueprint_thumbs/` again, and now hands out the ones it holds *For you: Switching between workspaces is quicker on a project with many pieces, because the piece pictures are not reloaded from disk on every switch.* *No clip: work counted headless; proved by tests/a_workspace_switch_keeps_the_scene.rs (a_second_arrival_decodes_no_cached_portrait_again)*
- **644** tests: every wait in the editor's tests is bounded in frames, not seconds (`settle_support::Frames`), and still pauses between frames, so a loaded host steps the same frames more slowly instead of running out of seconds and failing like a product bug *No clip: tests only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by the 181 touched and helper-using modules run under family_all on bmb from a pinned archive (settle_support, index_waits and index_view_support carry no `Instant`; frame-budget and real-time measurements keep theirs; the rig bench's history tests pace the editor's clock by their frames, so an agent's reveal cannot land between a key and the step it is about)*
- **670** The interface-size limit and the bottom dock's room rule add the Kit's tab row as it is laid out at the running text size, not a fixed 35 px *For you: At large text sizes on Kit, the editor no longer lets the interface grow a little past what the window can hold; the row it allows for is the one Kit actually draws (49 units at 300% text, not 35).* *No clip: a layout measured headless; proved by tests/kits_tab_row_is_the_row_the_limit_adds.rs (the row table is what the Kit lays out at all eleven text sizes; at text 300% the limit is the Map's need, the dock and the Kit's measured row)*
- **670** A workspace with one panel folds its tab into the chrome bar, and the tab row under it is gone *For you: On every workspace except Kit, the workspace's name and the link to switch to another one now sit in the top bar, so the row they had of their own goes to the work area.* *No clip: a layout measured headless; proved by tests/a_one_panel_workspace_folds_its_tab_into_the_chrome_bar.rs (the Map names itself once above the body; every one-panel workspace has no row and its tab and switch link in the bar; Kit keeps its row)*
- **636** gate: every gate that compiles writes the jobs, test threads, target dir, cores and load it ran under to gate.log *No clip: gate only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by tools/loop/test_pre_push_gate_log.py (test_every_gate_that_compiles_says_what_it_ran_under, test_a_push_from_a_shell_that_exports_the_shared_dir_gates_in_its_own)*
- **626** gate: a merge that pays the full gate says which clean-merge condition it missed, and still runs the whole suite *No clip: gate only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by tools/loop/test_pre_push_gate_log.py (test_a_merge_the_fast_path_cannot_take_says_which_condition_it_missed)*
- **625** gate: gate.log splits the test step into compile and run seconds, from cargo's Finished line and nextest's Summary line *No clip: gate only; no editor behaviour changed; proved by tools/loop/test_pre_push_gate_log.py (test_the_test_step_is_split_into_compile_and_run)*
- **684** The editor draws one button: every command button in a panel is gooey's, painted by the skin and named by its words *For you: The buttons in the side panels (Play, Build, the notes and the agent's answers, the Meshes tab's cell commands and the rest) now look and react like the buttons on the home screen and in Settings, and follow the theme the same way.* *No clip: a widget swap read headless; proved by tests/the_editor_has_one_button.rs and the suites that press these buttons (light_buttons, environment_applies, the_game_workspace_runs_the_game, a_wish_is_opened_or_discarded_in_the_agent_block)*
- **677** `frame_ms` is the whole frame and `busy_ms` the editor's share of it, from one clock, so they no longer agree by construction *For you: The frame profile and the editor's state now say how long a frame took and how much of that was the editor's own work, so a slow frame can be told from a slow machine or display.* *No clip: a clock measured headless; proved by tests/the_state_carries_a_frame_clock.rs (a 40 ms wait between frames is in frame_ms and not in busy_ms)*
- **678** The frame profile names the schedule each frame spent its time in, a workspace switch's included *For you: The frame profile lists where a frame's time went — state changes, updates, layout — so a slow workspace switch shows what it was doing.* *No clip: a split measured headless; proved by tests/a_workspace_switch_keeps_the_scene.rs (a switch's frame names StateTransition with its time)*
- **610** Code colours offers Monokai, Solarized and GitHub beside Skin: named palettes laid on the skin, each keyword drawn in its palette's published hue in YAML, RON, Rhai and Markdown *For you: Settings → Appearance → Code colours now lists Monokai, Solarized and GitHub; an open file recolours as you choose, Skin puts the theme's own colours back, and every palette stays readable on dark, light and high-contrast themes.* *No clip: Settings and a recolour, no new still; proved by the_code_colours_reach_an_open_editor::a_named_palette_draws_its_keyword_hue_in_every_language_and_skin_puts_it_back, the_code_colours_hold_the_contrast_bar::every_named_palette_keeps_its_keyword_hue_on_every_theme and the_code_colours_are_a_setting*
- **611** A Rust file open in Files shows rust-analyzer's compiler errors on their line, and a machine without rust-analyzer is told so once *For you: Open a .rs file in a Cargo project and its compile errors (a mismatched type, say) are underlined where they stand, from rust-analyzer; without rust-analyzer installed, the status line says so once with the command that installs it, and the file still opens, colours and takes typing.* *No clip: a server's findings read back from the editor's diagnostics headless; proved by tests/a_rust_files_compiler_errors_are_marked_by_rust_analyzer.rs (a type error marked on line 2 over the string, within a frame bound; the absent server said once, the file still coloured and typed in)*
- **694** Project, Sound, Game and Agent give their panel the centre instead of a dock beside a black viewport *For you: On the Project, Sound, Game and Agent workspaces the panel now fills the window's middle instead of sitting in a narrow column beside an empty black area.* *No clip: a layout measured headless; proved by tests/a_workspace_without_a_viewport_gives_its_panel_the_centre.rs (panel width and viewport per workspace)*
- **690** The toolbar is drawn only on Map, World and Kit, and no tool is lit where it cannot act *For you: Workspaces without the map's tools no longer show the toolbar, so their panels get its room; on World and Kit the tools that cannot act there are shown disabled.* *No clip: a layout measured headless; proved by tests/the_toolbar_follows_the_workspace.rs (census of all ten workspaces, the room given back, the armed tool lit on arrival)*
- **552** Every list row is gooey's `ListRow`: a selected row carries Bevy's `Selected` and the skin paints its fill and lights its rail, so the editor's second row painter (`RowSelected`, the rows' `RowRest`) is gone *For you: List rows (the palette, the Pieces lists, Notes, Compose, the console) now take the theme's own surface, hover and selected colours, so a selected row looks the same as everywhere else in the theme.* *No clip: a role swap read headless; proved by tests/every_selected_row_reads_at_aa_contrast_in_every_skin.rs and tests/the_files_trees_selected_row_reads_at_aa_in_every_skin.rs (a selected row reads at AA contrast in every skin)*
- **dev-incremental** Incremental dev builds: an edit to the editor's crate rebuilds its tests in about 27 s instead of about 255 s *No clip: a build-profile change nobody sees; proved by the rust-speed-audit 2026-11-26 measurement (254-259 s -> 26-28 s) and a passing gate*

## 2026-11-25

- **618** `d73cb2da` A closed comment issue's own grep is now a test, and four regressions it caught are fixed *No clip: a test and comment fixes; nobody can see this; proved by a_closed_comment_issues_grep_stays_clean*

## 2026-11-24

- **630** A source file the finder lists opens: the index's text-file test names the plain text kinds a project holds *For you: Ctrl+P now finds and opens a project's own code files (.rs, .py, .ts, .js, .sh, .csv), not only the kinds the editor colours* *No clip: an index row and a page, not a screen recording; proved by tests/a_listed_source_file_opens_from_the_finder.rs and tests/the_first_finder_query_searches_everything.rs*
- **598** Cmd+J cycles the terminal, and blueprint/state says which position *No clip: a state field plus a chord's measured cycle; proved by three_cmd_j_presses_cycle_the_pane_and_the_state_says_so*
- **599** The Files pane's field line says what it edits *No clip: one label on a field; proved by every_focusable_control_in_the_files_pane_is_labelled*
- **600** The Sound workspace draws its buses, cues and log on a default build *No clip: a build feature plus the panel it fills; proved by every_workspace_the_strip_offers_can_fill_itself*

- **578-a-timer-prunes-the-gate-target** `35f22e44` gate-prune.timer runs prune_gate_target.sh daily and holds ~/.cargo-target-gate to CEILING_GB (120), exiting 4 when it cannot; nothing scheduled the script before, and the target was 283 GB with 129.4 GB of superseded test executables cargo can never reuse *For you: The build folder that was filling the build machine now prunes itself every night.* *No clip: a systemd timer and a shell script, with no picture to show; proved by tools/loop/test_prune_gate_target.py (the ceiling path exits 4) and a --apply run that freed 129.4 GB on 2026-11-23*
- **577-the-filter-names-the-tests** `37498c73` nextest's num-cpus override names the 23 tests that actually read a frame budget instead of the six modules they live in (28 tests owned all four slots for 52.7 s of gate #10's 581.8 s, 9 of them reading no clock), and tools/loop/wall_clock_filter.py fails when a clock-reading test is unnamed or a named one reads none *For you: The test suite stops making tests that measure nothing take the whole machine.* *No clip: a nextest config and a checker, with no picture to show; proved by tools/loop/test_wall_clock_filter.py and a nextest run at NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS=4*
- **575-the-gate-record-travels** `a7c5b553` The clean-merge fast path's record is a refs/notes/gated note pushed with the commit instead of a per-clone gate-passed file, so a merge of two commits gated on different hosts can satisfy it (it fired on 5 of 24 clean merges before) *For you: A clean merge of two already-checked commits now skips the test run on any machine, not only the one that checked them.* *No clip: a hook, with nothing to show; proved by tools/loop/test_pre_push_clippy_memo.py*
- **574-a-refused-gate-keeps-its-clippy** `60d855ba` A tree that linted clean is not linted again: gate-clippy holds one line per (tree, toolchain, RUSTFLAGS), read by both the full and the clean-merge path, so a push refused at the test step no longer pays clippy twice (1,149 s over 30 of 161 commits in the log) *For you: A push rejected for a flaky test no longer pays to re-lint code it already linted.* *No clip: a hook, with nothing to show; proved by tools/loop/test_pre_push_clippy_memo.py*
- **573-ci-target-in-the-slot-cache** `b18dfb8d` CI's target lives in the runner's slot cache (CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/cache/target, a volume actions/checkout never cleans) and a run prints the Cargo.lock/toolchain/RUSTFLAGS triple that differed from the last, so a 5.3x cold build names its cause; a cargo-slot queue reports its own wait line *For you: The weekly check no longer rebuilds the whole project because a folder inside the checkout was cleaned.* *No clip: a workflow and its log, with no picture to show; proved by the steps in .github/workflows/ci.yml and the cold-run numbers in #573*
- **572-ci-fails-on-a-red-test** `02481cca` The CI job fails when a compile or a test fails (both test steps set pipefail, so a nonzero cargo status is the step's; six runs had concluded success with '3 failed' in their own uploaded log) *For you: The daily check can now go red when the code is broken, instead of reporting success.* *No clip: a workflow and its exit status, with no picture to show; proved by a workflow_dispatch on a commit with a deliberately failing test concluding failure while test-output.txt is still uploaded*

- **501-world-lists** `052940c3` the World and Sound lists read the project index and no longer re-read their files twice a second: world_lists::READ_SECS is gone; a file is read when its stamp moves, a change to a file no list shows reads nothing, and a file the index does not list is said in the row rather than read *For you: A change to the water, forest or sound files from outside, such as an agent writing one, shows in the World and Sound lists within about a second.* *No clip: no visible difference when the change arrives; proved by the_world_lists_follow_the_project_index*
- **501-notes-view** `e903eb70` The notes view reads the map's notes file when the project index says it moved, not on its own timer (notes_view::RECHECK_SECS is gone), and the notes operations tell the index they wrote it, so a note the editor adds shows within a second even with the index's sweep ten minutes apart With the index's default 1 s sweep an edit can be seen up to about a second later than the per-frame stat showed it [INFERENCE: sweep plus debounce, not measured end to end]; the stamp is length and modification time, so two writes of the same length inside one tick leave the same stamp and are no longer caught (the old once-a-second re-read caught them). *For you: A change made to the notes file from outside shows within about a second, and a note you add in the editor shows at once.* *No clip: not visible: no screen change; proved by the_notes_view_follows_the_project_index*
- **501-oplog-view** `718f4c5e` The operation-log view reads oplog.jsonl when the project index says it moved, not on its own timer (oplog_view::RECHECK_SECS is gone); an append is one Modified event and one read of the whole file (a 45 ms frame at 5,000 lines, 431 ms at 50,000, measured on big), and an undo, which reads the log itself, was not delayed: 75 us and 86 us right after an operation With the index's default 1 s sweep an edit can be seen up to about a second later than the per-frame stat showed it [INFERENCE: sweep plus debounce, not measured end to end]; the stamp is length and modification time, so two writes of the same length inside one tick leave the same stamp and are no longer caught (the old once-a-second re-read caught them). *For you: A damaged line in the operation log is named within about a second of the edit, and undo is never held up by that watching.* *No clip: not visible: no screen change; proved by the_oplog_view_follows_the_project_index*
- **501-checks-view** `ec7daa19` The checks view reads checks.yaml when the project index says it moved, not on its own timer (checks_view::RECHECK_SECS is gone), and refuses a file the index cannot announce (named by .gitignore, a symbolic link, outside the project); a neighbour changing makes it do no filesystem look With the index's default 1 s sweep an edit can be seen up to about a second later than the per-frame stat showed it [INFERENCE: sweep plus debounce, not measured end to end]; the stamp is length and modification time, so two writes of the same length inside one tick leave the same stamp and are no longer caught (the old once-a-second re-read caught them). *For you: A change made to the project's checks file from outside shows within about a second, and a checks file that can't be watched is named instead of ignored.* *No clip: not visible: no screen change; proved by the_checks_view_follows_the_project_index*
- **501-data-view** `f4f9e605` The data view reads data.yaml and the files it lists when the project index says one moved, not on its own once-a-second timer (data_view::RECHECK_SECS is gone), and refuses a registered file the index cannot announce; a file beside them that changes makes it do no filesystem look; with the index's default 1 s sweep an edit is seen up to about a second later than the per-frame stat showed it [INFERENCE] *For you: A change made to a game's data files from outside shows within about a second, and a data file that can't be watched is named instead of ignored.* *No clip: not visible: no screen change; proved by the_data_view_follows_the_project_index*
- **494-lists-break-in-their-rows** `a6914d07` Category headings in the PLACE list, the reason on a TILES row that cannot open, COMPOSE group and member rows, and RIGS names now break inside their rows instead of being drawn past the panel's border (the piece rows already did, #468): at the capture's window and text 100% the `FURNITURE · KIND_TOKEN_...` heading stood 292.8 px past the content edge and the longer `AN_EXTRA_KIT_...` heading 481.4 px (two headings in the same cell, hence two numbers), the TILES reason 53 px, a RIGS name 392 px, and a COMPOSE row 30 px at interface 0.5 *For you: Long names in the group, tile and rig lists now wrap inside their panels instead of spilling over the edge.* *No clip: a layout measurement with no clip recorded; measured by the populated census at the capture's window (2560 x 1604, 2.0 display factor); proved by tests/a_panel_census_at_five_scales_over_populated_content.rs and tests/a_short_row_stands_where_it_stood.rs*
- **501-index** `3dfb5c19` one background thread now walks the project once and publishes each changed file once, and the finder's scan and the file tree read what it holds, so two of the 22 poll constants (crosslinks::SCAN_POLL_S and files::TREE_POLL_S) are gone; 20 modules still stat on their own clocks, the listing is an error rather than a second walk when no index can answer, and the finder's scan still runs on the UI thread (4 ms at 10,000 files, 43 ms at 100,000) whenever a text file comes, goes or changes *No clip: no visible surface; stage 1 of #501, proved by crates/bevy_dark_factory/tests/the_project_index_is_the_one_watcher.rs and the_frame_a_change_lands_on_stays_inside_two_milliseconds.rs*
- **497** `88b481d3` At text scale 3.0 the terminal bar clips instead of pushing the pane past the window, and keeps its 24 px height, so the Start agent button can be clicked *For you: With very large text, the terminal no longer pushes its Start agent button off the window.* *No clip: no visible clip needed; proved by the_leds_word_is_the_size_of_a_chip_in_the_bar_and_of_a_row_on_the_card_at_every_text_size and the bar-height check in shift_primary_j_covers_the_window_below_the_menu_bar_and_again_gives_the_height_back*

- **484-session-servers** `f37b3a00` The config the editor writes for an agent session carries the editor's own MCP server plus the servers the open project declares in its .mcp.json, and the self-test card names the servers written for the session; a machine-level server is not in that file. omp still loads its own machine-level servers beside it, which needs an upstream allow-list (#484 on can1357/oh-my-pi#19573) *For you: The agent you start from the editor is handed the editor's tools and whatever your project declares, and the self-test card lists the servers it was started with.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **477-agent-model** `7e8842d1` The agent tab opens omp on the model a person names in Settings (terminal_agent_model), in both the fresh and the resume form, and its self-test card says which model is answering; a model omp refuses fails the Agent connected row naming the id rather than falling back *For you: The agent the editor starts now runs the model you picked once in your settings, so every editor you open talks to the same model, and the self-test card tells you which one.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **panel-heading-centre** `9f4ca82780` A panel heading's ? sits on the title's centre line, inside its pill *For you: The help ? beside a panel's name now lines up with the name instead of sitting high and off its button.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by a_panel_headings_glyph_sits_on_the_titles_line*
- **left-pane-reflows** `956eb623b6` The left pane's rows reflow when the divider is dragged left *For you: Narrowing the left panel no longer pushes its buttons over the map's divider.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_left_pane_reflows_when_the_divider_moves (2 tests)*
- **split-divider-seam** `752fefdd74` The split divider's thickness is named in one constant, and the knob to make it a 12 px seam is filed *No clip: the visible change is owed by bevy_gooey#108, so nothing a person sees changed yet; proved by the_split_divider_is_a_seam_not_a_bar (2 tests)*
- **480-chrome-readouts** `49ed5c7f02` The chrome bar's readouts are drawn a step smaller than the menu's words so the band of state reads as a strip, not a second menu row *For you: The strip under the menu now looks like readouts rather than a second menu.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **488-panel-authoring** `92b51ec881` A panel's authoring format is researched and recommended: the typed schema is the source of truth, YAML is its text form, and Markdown stays prose *No clip: a design document and its measured evidence; no code changed, so nothing in the editor's suite can fail on it -- see docs/design/2026-11-17-a-panels-authoring-format.md*
- **play-refusal-readable** `1bff932e` A refusal's strip is as tall as the refusal: the play.yaml message shows its whole YAML body and its trailing sentence instead of being cut at the strip's height (#479) *For you: When a project has no play.yaml the editor now shows the whole message telling you how to write one, instead of cutting off the part that tells you what it is for.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **495-capture-files-its-issue** `5d531fed86` A Cmd+D capture becomes a GitHub issue from the editor: the seven-section body, both pictures embedded, filed only after their URLs answer 200 *For you: A capture you take with Cmd+D can now become a real GitHub issue with its pictures, without you touching git.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **485-interface-scale-chords** `a478a6f54c` The whole interface steps its size on Cmd+= and Cmd+-, and Cmd+Shift+0 puts it back to 100% *For you: Grow or shrink the whole editor with the zoom keys you already use in a browser.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **490-view-depth-covers-the-ground** `b9917938e4` The Map camera's depth range covers the ground the map actually draws, so WASD panning no longer cuts a straight edge across the viewport *For you: Panning the map no longer cuts the ground away at a straight line, so what you see is the whole map wherever you walk to.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*

## 2026-11-16

- **474-volume-edit-corrects** volume_edit corrects an entry: at: <index> replaces what stands there and remove: <index> takes one out, each one logged undoable change, and an index the list has not got is refused naming it and the list's length instead of appending silently *For you: A carve that came out wrong can be corrected in place, so fixing a room no longer means hand-editing the file in a way the undo history never saw.* *No clip: a parameter and its refusal; proved by volume_edit_can_correct_an_entry*
- **473-volume-edit-says-which** volume_edit names the entry it changed: the index it stands at, how many entries the list holds, the shape and mode, and for a replace or a removal what stood there, in the receipt and in the log line's after *For you: When an agent carves a room it now says which entry of the map's carve list it wrote, instead of just 'edited'.* *No clip: an answer's words and a log field; proved by volume_edit_says_which_entry_it_changed*
- **dm-455-pplx-exclusion** pplx-decider's calibration record says 121 of 122 samples and names the one image the provider's scanner refused, so a row never claims more of the set than it measured (#455) *No clip: calibration records and a policy; proved by an_excluded_sample_is_stated_in_the_record.rs and docs/decide/models.md*
- **dm-455-pplx-row** The pplx-decider row of docs/decide/models.md is measured where it can be: the fixture's three answers, 418 input tokens, 0.66 s and cost 0; its clip-set agreement is blocked and named, not guessed (#455) *No clip: calibration records and a measurement; proved by a_snapshot_is_the_model_it_names.rs, a_refused_sample_is_named.rs and docs/decide/models.md*
- **dm-453-openrouter-recorded** The OpenRouter Decisions exchange is recorded and replays with no key: pplx-decider answers the fixture in 0.66 s for 418 input tokens at cost 0, the free tier's pace is held by the calibration run, and a refused sample names itself (#453) *No clip: a transport and its fixture; proved by crates/bevy_oracle/tests/openrouter.rs (the replay, which now fails rather than skips without the fixture), the_free_tier_is_paced_by_the_caller.rs, a_snapshot_is_the_model_it_names.rs and a_refused_sample_is_named.rs*
- **bm-06-omp-real-run** A real omp run and a matrix of two models, on the BM-01 example tasks: tokens and tool calls read from omp's own transcript, one report with both rows and the frontier marked *No clip: a comparison report; proved by docs/bench/runs/2026-11-17-omp-real.md, docs/bench/matrices/2026-11-17-bench-omp.md and a_matrix_of_pairs_writes_one_report_with_every_row_and_the_frontier_marked*
- **bench-advisory** `PIN` The benchmark is printed beside a release and no longer refuses it: a release is not gated on a run while the editor is built out (BM-05 stays as a finding, not a stop) *No clip: a release-process change; proved by the workflow step calling bench_advisory.sh and the check's own tests*
- **dl-01-result-not-question** A turn's after picture is taken with the Keep / Undo question out of its frame, and the record's `render.question_open` says whether a question was really on the picture, so the label source is the agent's result rather than the dialog about it (#471) *For you: The picture saved of what an agent made now shows the work itself rather than the Keep or Undo question standing over it.* *No clip: visible only in the pictures a ledger holds; proved by the_after_picture_has_no_question_on_it*
- **dl-02-agents-answer** An answer an agent pressed for itself is no longer recorded as the person's gold verdict on the turn: the frame's input carries whose hands are on it — the injected pointer and a chord `blueprint/run_command` queued say an agent, a person's keyboard and clicks stay gold (#470) *For you: An agent that answers its own hand-back no longer counts as you having judged the result, so what you keep or undo is the only thing the training data learns from.* *No clip: visible only as a label that is no longer written; proved by an_agents_answer_is_never_a_gold_label*
- **pin-105-band** `PIN` The gooey pin moves to the band fix: a Pieces band grows to hold a wrapped pack name instead of spilling over the next row (#468, bevy_gooey#105) *No clip: a dependency pin bump; proved by the editor's own suite and gooey's band_scroll.rs, which fails without the fix*
- **dm-06-guide-revised** The guide asks what the run showed pays: one still after every visible change, only the questions whose answers could change your next operation (the thing asked for is there, its middle clear, the shape asked for, nothing floating — plus one per amount or kind the request names), and a low answer read per question type (a noul under 0.5, a choice whose winning option is, a score in its lowest level), fixed and asked again, and handed back with its number if one retry does not clear it; the four-or-five demand is gone, and the retry rule now has that exit instead of a bare twice cap (#457). Owner exception (2026-11-14): a deliberate change of this issue's own guide, so `the_guide_asks_by_looking` re-pins its needle list (three retired demands asserted absent; the new rules required by name) and `decide_look_then_ask`'s module doc no longer says four or five — no assertion removed or loosened *For you: Agents now ask the question whose answer would change what they do next, and fix what comes back low before going on.* *No clip: a guide's wording; proved by the_guide_says_to_check_your_work_by_asking_and_says_how and the #457 measurements in docs/bench/2026-11-15-ask-experiment.md*
- **dl-08-review-fixes** The label report counts only the holdout answers it compared before opening escalation, counts a revision as the Undo it is, and its workspace line counts engine-made defects as the report does (#467) *No clip: a report and its line; proved by the_label_report_says_what_the_set_is_worth*
- **dl-08-label-report** The label report: per week and per question, the labels per tier, the class balance per source, the assessor's agreement with the person (its errors through an Undo, and the holdout), the fast model's against both, and what escalation saved; plus dark_factory-labels report writing report.md and report.json beside an export, byte-identical between runs (#467) *No clip: a report in its own process, and one line in the Agent workspace; proved by the_label_report_says_what_the_set_is_worth*
- **dm-06-guide-revised** The guide asks what the run showed pays: one still after every visible change, only the questions whose answers could change your next operation (the four that discriminate — the thing asked for is there, its middle is clear, it is the shape asked for, nothing floats — plus one per amount or kind named), and an answer under 0.5 fixed and the same question asked again; the four-or-five demand and the twice cap are gone (#457) *For you: Agents now ask the question whose answer would change what they do next, and fix what comes back under half before going on.* *No clip: a guide's wording; proved by the_guide_says_to_check_your_work_by_asking_and_says_how and the #457 measurements in docs/bench/2026-11-15-ask-experiment.md*
- **dl-06-review-fixes** The label export refuses a ledger line that does not read and an oldest build that is not a version, shows both pictures to a question about the change, and counts only the assessor's contradictions as the teacher's errors (#465) *No clip: an export in its own process, not visible; proved by the_tiers_resolve_into_a_valen_set*

- **dl-06-valen-export** The label tiers are resolved and the ledger exports as Valen's training JSONL, split by scene: gold wins, the engine answers its own checks, a teacher's votes are used only where they agree, everything else is parked and counted, and the human holdout is its own file (#465) *No clip: an export in its own process, not visible; proved by the_tiers_resolve_into_a_valen_set and Valen's own reader (valen/data/schema.py read_jsonl) loading the file*

- **dm-454-review-follow-up** `6be337f8` Review follow-up for the decision-model setting: the LED's words and docs say the top bar, the LED paint borrows its state, the preflight never dials Remote, and a Tracked transport is tested against a stand-in server and a closed port (#454) *No clip: docs and a refactor; proved by decision_model::tests::tracked_send_records_the_answer_and_the_closed_port*
- **469-prose-face** `aee8076ed` Prose is drawn in Fira Sans and everything else stays FiraMono: each What's new sentence and the Learn page's introduction read in a proportional face (SIL OFL 1.1, licence beside it), keys, ids, paths, tables, the Inspector and the terminal keep their monospace columns, and the What's new column is 380 px so a sentence is at most 75 glyphs a line (#469, option 2)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-16-469-prose-face.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-16-469-prose-face.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **468-pieces-text-inside** `26a9d8a48` The Pieces panel's hint (right: meshes / left: tiles) has a line of its own and wraps, a long file name breaks at a letter, and a pack's band grows to its wrapped name so no two rows' words are drawn over each other (#468; the band fix is bevy_gooey#105 at the f1904be pin)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-16-468-pieces-text-inside.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-16-468-pieces-text-inside.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **dm-06-ask-measured** `1df66667aa2118b8a8609e6e48f3db8829e8e51a` Agents' asking is measured: with DM-05's guide an agent asks with four or more questions in 51% of eligible trials (target 80%, missed) against 0% without it, and task success is 92% against 95%, no measurable change (#457) *No clip: a measurement; proved by docs/bench/runs/2026-11-15-ask-with-guide.md and 2026-11-16-ask-without-guide.md*
- **dl-07-audit** `2729175b` The Agent workspace offers an Audit row: a few of the questions about what the agent did, answered with Y, N or S, kept as gold labels that are never trained on (#466) *For you: A few questions about what the agent did wait in the agent panel, and one key answers each one: yes, no, or skip.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-16-466-audit-row.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-16-466-audit-row.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **dl-04-assessor** `3eb2422d` dark_factory-labels assess grades each recorded turn's questions three times each with a fresh-context model, and records the votes as silver labels (#463) *No clip: a batch job in its own process, not visible; proved by the_assessor_grades_each_turns_questions*
- **dl-01-review-2** `f23928b8` The labels ledger takes a picture only of the Map or the World, cut to the map camera's own viewport, and keeps no key in a decision error; the Learn from my use row is under Decisions (#460) *No clip: a ledger written by the editor, not visible; proved by a_hand_back_while_the_editor_is_on_files_records_no_picture and an_error_that_names_the_endpoint_carries_no_key*
- **dm-455-calibration-per-model** `ea836efb` A calibration belongs to one model: a check carries one record per model, a finding names the model that answered and says uncalibrated for it (advisory, never blocking), a calibration run refuses an endpoint that serves another model, and d3-flash agrees 0.861 on the clip set against clef-flash's 0.533 (#455) *No clip: calibration records and a policy; proved by crates/bevy_oracle/tests/a_calibration_belongs_to_one_model.rs, the_clip_check_is_scored_against_its_problems.rs, the_per_model_calibrations_are_recorded.rs and docs/decide/models.md*
- **dm-454-decision-model-setting** `eeada65b` One decision-model setting, Local or Remote, in Settings: the editor, its status LED, the labeller, the after-edit checks, blueprint_decide and the agent self test all read it (#454). Migration: the BLUEPRINT_DECIDE_* environment variables and the project .env that held them are retired, with no compatibility read; OPENROUTER_API_KEY stays in the environment *For you: Settings now has one Decision model choice, Local or Remote, and the editor shows which model is in use and whether it answers.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-15-dm-454-decision-model-setting.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-15-dm-454-decision-model-setting.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>

- **dm-456-look-then-ask** `322634b87` An agent checks its work by asking: blueprint_decide with view: true takes the still, answers four or five questions with the model, where it ran and the latency, and accepts a capture_still's path or fingerprint (#456) *For you: Agents now check what they made by asking a decision model about a fresh still before they hand back.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **dm-01-d3-flash** `e1019ed8` d3-flash answers decisions on big's GPU for every editor on the LAN: bevy_oracle's default endpoint is http://big.local:8081/upstream/d3-flash/v1/systemone, a lease evicts the model and a request then names it unreachable with the remedy, and a name is resolved over IPv4 (#452) *No clip: a served model; proved by the A.4 checks recorded in docs/decide/serving-d3-flash.md and a_gateway_that_cannot_run_the_model_is_unreachable_with_the_remedy, a_name_is_resolved_to_its_ipv4_address_only*

- **dl-02-review-fixes** `351de082` A turn the person kept is not marked corrected by a follow-up, an edit labels only the newest turn that covers it, and an Undo all that stops part way is still recorded (#461) *No clip: labels in a ledger; proved by the_persons_verdicts_become_labels*

- **dl-03-turn-questions** `e2b3b78d` Each turn's record carries the typed questions a student will be asked about it: one declarative question per specified line, the named checks with the engine's answer, the agent's own questions, and whether the person keeps the result (#462) *No clip: questions stored in a record, not visible; proved by the_turns_carry_their_questions*

- **core-0-12-upgrade** Upgrading blueprint_model from 0.11 to 0.12: the references module is removed (the board module replaces it: every place of the design tree has a dream board at <place>/board/board.yaml, and bevy_dark_factory migrate moves an old references file to the level's board with the same captions), trust::GuideGrant is removed (one trust::TextGrant per file replaces it: use TrustStore::text_decision, trust_text and revoke_text; a version 1 or 2 trust store is still read, so what a person allowed stays allowed), and a style module is added *No clip: an API change in a library crate, written down for the release; proved by the_design_tree_is_in_the_editors_state, a_dream_board_for_every_place and the trust tests that read a version 2 store*
- **docs-factory-readmes** `c66761fe` The README, the guide, PROJECT.md and every crate README describe the editor as it is: ten workspaces, the Design workspace and style, the terminal, the hand-back and wishes, the benchmark; blueprint_model's README says what 0.12.0 breaks (the references module is replaced by board, trust::GuideGrant by TextGrant) *No clip: a README and docs change; proved by every relative and own-repository link in the touched documents resolving, docs/mcp-tools.md matching the server's own --emit-docs, and the rng and solver README doctests passing*
