- **vis-maximised-terminal-over-overlays** `44fc9d77` A maximised terminal is painted over the map's axis gizmo and overlays (#435)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-visual-fixes-terminal.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-visual-fixes-terminal.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **placement-add-refuses-an-overlap** `77c9ed97` An agent's placement_add onto an occupied footprint is refused naming both pieces unless it passes replace: true; a replacing add names what it replaced and the hand-back card lists it as a replacement; the click still replaces (#443) *No clip: an API an agent calls; proved by placement_add_refuses_an_overlap_unless_told_to_replace and the_guide_says_placement_add_refuses_an_overlap*
- **one-operations-help** `81f10a58` blueprint_list_operations { operation } answers for one operation with its parameters and an example, under 2 KB for data_set instead of the 70 KB list, and blueprint_style's refusal of an unknown topic lists the topics that exist (#444) *No clip: an API an agent calls; proved by an_agent_can_ask_for_one_operations_help, a_refused_topic_lists_the_topics_that_exist and blueprint_list_operations_can_ask_for_one_operation*
- **sg-10-style-replication** `66ae03740` The style measurement was replicated on the fixed runner with all 30 runs scored: the with-style agent satisfied 24 of 24 style lines, the without-style agent 10.4, and the harness-error attrition of the first run is gone (SG-10, #409) *No clip: a measurement; proved by the replication report and the registered analysis script run on its raw data*
- **sg-05-a-levels-style-in-three-presses** `615fe451a` A level's style is a measured destination: Cmd/Ctrl+0 and one click on its row, two actions of the three allowed (SG-05, #404) *No clip: a measurement row; proved by navigation::every_destination_is_measured_and_the_baseline_says_so*
- **ui-100-the-journal-is-gooeys-notification-centre** `eaee00b10` The journal on Cmd+E is gooey's notification centre: every step and refusal is a row, the refusal toast is the same row, and the newest step that can be taken back has an Undo button, which takes the step back as the person (even while the agent is paused), and a journal longer than the log's 200 rows says how many older lines it left out (UI-100, #442) *For you: Press Cmd+E and the list of everything that happened is drawn by the interface library now: the newest line is first, each line starts with the time (such as 0:42), a refusal starts with an exclamation mark, the old STEPS heading and the indent are gone, and the newest change you or your agent made has an Undo button beside it.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-ui-100-the-journal-is-gooeys-notification-centre.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-ui-100-the-journal-is-gooeys-notification-centre.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-99-gooey-g92** `aeb23e871` The terminal's pane root and self-test card are laid out with bevy_gooey instead of raw nodes: the editor has no hand-built node left outside the viewport (#260 done) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted and the_g91_the_pane_root_and_the_agent_card_are_gooeys*
- **terminal-find-box-opens** `e3cda3e9` Opening the terminal's find box no longer crashes on Linux (a duplicate Pickable in its spawn), and LinesRoom is retired (#260 review) *No clip: a crash fix behind an existing key; proved by the_terminal_finds_in_its_scrollback and the_terminal_overlays_block_the_clicks_on_their_own_ground*
- **ui-99-gooey-g91** `02f602e4` The editor's panels are bevy_gooey boxes whose width the editor writes through the interface library (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: no visible change intended; proved by the_g89_nodes_are_gooeys and the whole editor binary*
- **ui-99-gooey-g90** `ab6eaef6` Every scrolling list in the editor is bevy_gooey's scroll viewport instead of a hand-built scroll container (UI-99, #260 part) *For you: Lists scroll to the focused row and answer Page Up, Page Down, Home and End, and the scrollbar beside them is the interface library's now rather than the editor's own thumb.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_g90_scroll_viewport_is_the_list and the protected scroll tests*
- **ui-99-gooey-g89** `651058b1` The error journal, the bottom dock and the terminal's grip, bar, body, caret and rows are built from bevy_gooey's closed-at-start, placed and host-geometry boxes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: no visible change intended; proved by the_g89_nodes_are_gooeys and the whole editor binary*
- **ui-99-gooey-g88** `91069f87` The shell split, the receipt row, the terminal's find box and invitation are built from bevy_gooey's filling split, rounded-up line extent and placed layers; the unused modal card is gone (UI-99, #260 part) *For you: The room kept for the receipt line under the map's size fields is a third of a pixel smaller than before, and never short of two lines of text.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_g87_nodes_are_gooeys and the whole editor binary*
- **ui-99-gooey-g87** `bd8bf289` The frame, the chips, the cards, the name box, the toast strip and the key badges are built from bevy_gooey's boxes, gaps and host-owned colours instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *For you: The name box, the toast strip, the label progress and the stage labels hide through the interface library now, so one that is not showing can never take the keyboard focus.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_g87_nodes_are_gooeys and the whole editor binary*
- **sg-10-style-results** `342297f61` The style measurement ran on the sample game: with blueprint_style the agent satisfied 24 of 24 style lines, without it 10.5, on 20 and 10 scored runs after 18 of 48 runs were harness errors (SG-10, #409) *No clip: a measurement; proved by the results report and the registered analysis script run on its raw data*
- **bm-11-a-mangled-tool-name-is-an-agent-error** `8463f880` A benchmark trial whose agent calls a tool that does not exist (omp refuses the name and the agent goes on) is scored and the report counts it as an agent error, where the runner threw it away as a harness error and the style study lost 18 of 48 trials to it; a tool outside the set that actually ran is still a harness error, and the editor tool called by its own name is counted (BM-11, #441) *No clip: a benchmark runner fix; proved by a_mangled_tool_name_is_the_agents_mistake and a_report_lists_agent_errors_per_arm*
- **terminal-hangup-wait-is-listed** `182c345d` Closing a terminal lists its wait for the shell to hang up for the exit watchdog, so a quit stuck there names it (#337) *No clip: no visible change; proved by a_terminals_hangup_wait_is_listed_for_the_exit_watchdog and closing_a_terminal_never_waits_on_its_child*
- **terminal-colours-follow-the-theme** `ff45cb42` A terminal's default text and ground, and its sixteen ANSI colours, follow a theme change the frame it lands, without the program redrawing; a 24-bit colour a program wrote keeps its value (#428, #421) *For you: Choosing a theme in Settings now recolours the text already in the terminal at once, instead of leaving it in the old theme's colours until the program redraws.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-ui-theme-moves-every-colour.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-ui-theme-moves-every-colour.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **settings-agent-row-follows-its-state** `4be6dd85` The Terminal agent row's problem line, its Use Oh My Pi button and its command field follow the settings: choosing Custom opens the command field, a Custom with no command says so, and setting a command clears it, while every other setting still changes in place (#425, #429) *For you: In Settings, the Terminal agent row now shows its problem and its command field as soon as they apply, instead of staying as they were when the screen opened.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_terminal_agent_row_follows_its_state_in_place and a_custom_agent_without_a_command_is_a_settings_problem*
- **bm-10-eval-calls-are-seen** `327d2fad` A benchmark trial in which an omp agent answers through its eval tool is no longer scored as having made no hand-back: the runner counts the calls the eval made, reads the hand-back from the editor's state, and calls a trial it cannot read a harness error; the report's cost includes the pre-flight turn, the run says which environment variables it withheld, and dark_factory-bench clean removes the credential directories older runs left *No clip: a runner and editor-state fix; proved by an_eval_routed_call_is_seen, an_unseen_hand_back_is_graded_from_the_editor, the_editors_state_holds_the_hand_back, a_report_counts_the_preflight_turn, a_clean_removes_stale_trial_homes and a_run_says_which_variables_it_withholds*
- **sg-10-file-value-strings** `ea179c404` The benchmark can check a saved string by the words it holds (file_value contains and lacks), so the style measurement scores the greeting an agent saved and not what it said about it (SG-10, #409 part 1) *No clip: a benchmark check; proved by a_data_file_string_is_checked_by_what_it_says and the_greeting_task_is_scored_on_the_saved_leaf*
- **sg-10-style-suite** `ec8642dd3` The sample game has a style of two pages and a three-task benchmark suite with a with-style and a without-style arm, ready to measure whether blueprint_style helps an agent (SG-10, #409 part 1) *No clip: a task suite and the style it scores; proved by dark_factory-bench --agent nothing failing and --agent oracle passing all three tasks on a real editor, and by the_style_suite_scores_every_line_once and the_sample_games_style_states_its_lines*
- **ui-99-gooey-review-7** `6ad38f5d` The compass's arms and dots keep their size on bevy_gooey's host geometry, and the review's pins hold the button boxes, the thumbnail slot and the rails (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: fix of a regression this branch introduced before release; proved by the_round_7_review_is_pinned*
- **ui-theme-moves-every-colour** `11bc6f18` A theme changes every colour the editor draws, live: the headings, rows, chips, status band, panel edges and the window behind them follow Tool dark, Parchment or High contrast the frame the setting lands, and Parchment is no longer refused (#428) *For you: Choosing Parchment or High contrast in Settings now recolours the editor's own screens at once, with no restart: the headings, the rows and buttons, the status band and the panel edges change with the theme, not only the widgets, and Parchment, which the editor used to refuse, is a readable cream theme. Thumbnails already baked keep their old backdrop until they are rebaked.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-ui-theme-moves-every-colour.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-ui-theme-moves-every-colour.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-99-gooey-chrome** `b5232e5e` The dialogs' card, the severity rails, the activity bar, the auto chip and the key badges' layer use bevy_gooey's columns, exact px borders and Shown instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *For you: When a row of options in Settings or in the Tiles layers wraps onto a second line, the lines now stand 16 px apart (they stood 0 and 5 px apart before), and the snip rectangle, the document tabs and the report outline keep their coloured edges.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_borders_and_paddings_are_gooeys and the whole editor binary*
- **ui-99-gooey-button-boxes** `06ea713c` The project cards, map cards, what's-new entries and kit rows are bevy_gooey button boxes instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *For you: A map card, a what's-new entry and a kit row can be reached with Tab like a project card now.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_cards_and_rows_are_button_boxes and the launcher and project page tests*
- **ui-99-gooey-borders** `a15632f8` The snip rectangle, the report outline, the document tabs, the follow chip, the triangle readout, the jobs and wish status items and the compass use bevy_gooey's exact borders, px paddings, Shown and HostGeometry instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *For you: The band's job and wish notices are hidden through the interface library now, so a notice that is not showing can never take the keyboard focus.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_borders_and_paddings_are_gooeys and the snip, report and compass tests*
- **ui-99-gooey-regions** `68e99266` The Design page's regions, the file search block, the workspace strip's height, the review thumbnail and the welcome and news columns use bevy_gooey's fill_region, Sizing and column_with instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_gooey_swaps_keep_what_a_person_saw and the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted*
- **ui-99-gooey-textflow** `dd8f3e1f` Texts that take the room a row leaves or keep their width, the palette's cells and the outline holder use bevy_gooey's TextFlow, Sizing and Spacing instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; the measures are the ones they replace: proved by the_agent_blocks_lines_are_gooey_rows and the wish, palette and outline tests*
- **ui-99-gooey-placed** `64bb6950` The speech balloon, the snip label and the report's covering layers, marks and hint are placed with bevy_gooey's placed and cover instead of hand-built absolute nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_placed_nodes_are_gooeys and the snip and report tests*
- **ui-99-gooey-pages** `1cc9ede6` The home's pages, the loading card and the editor's spacers use bevy_gooey's Shown, fill_region_with and spacer instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *For you: The home's Learn and What's new pages are hidden through the interface library now, so nothing in a page you are not on can take the keyboard focus.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; proved by the_pages_and_spacers_are_gooeys*
- **ui-99-gooey-spacing** `ce63386c` The editor's margins and paddings (headings, titles, button rows, the project page's grids) are gooey's Spacing in steps of the editor skin's scale instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; the steps equal the px they replace and the headings keep their margins and ink: proved by the_spacing_is_gooeys and the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted*
- **preparing-band-takes-no-clicks** `282bcdf12` The Preparing the view band no longer blocks clicks (#430) *For you: The Preparing the view note never gets in the way of your clicks.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **quit-question-is-never-ended** `b484b4eac` A quit that is waiting on the unsaved-edits question is not ended when frames pause, and a Cmd+Q pressed in another app does not count as a second press (#427) *For you: DarkFactory no longer force-quits while it is asking about unsaved changes.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **settings-hidden-list-keeps-scroll** `2dc114ece` A Settings list on a tab that is not shown keeps its scroll position, and a keyboard Reset puts the focus on the control of the tab you are on (#429) *For you: Switching Settings tabs no longer loses your place in the list.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **loading-prepares-the-view** `4d3adfedb` After a project opens, a quiet Preparing the view band stays up until the editor's frames are smooth again (10 frames in a row under 50 ms, never longer than 10 s) (#430) *For you: After you open a project, a small note says Preparing the view while the editor finishes getting ready.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **quit-armed-by-any-request** `80d5eae71` A quit request of any kind (Cmd+Q, File > Quit, the window close button) starts the quit watch: an app that stops drawing after one is ended after 3 s, and two presses of the quit key end an app that froze before it could read the first (#427) *For you: If DarkFactory freezes when you try to quit, press Cmd+Q twice and it closes.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **settings-reset-keeps-focus** `cff59b1b0` After a keyboard Reset in Settings the focus moves to the row's own control, and a list that got shorter at its end keeps its scroll instead of jumping when it grows back (#429) *For you: Resetting a setting from the keyboard keeps you on that setting.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **quit-second-press-ends-a-stuck-quit** `30530589c57c61988da6867bb35a7afd10388321` A quit that gets stuck names the wait it is stuck in, and pressing the quit key again after 0.8 s ends the process at once (#427) *For you: If quitting gets stuck, press Cmd+Q again after a moment and DarkFactory closes at once.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **loading-says-building-the-editor** `028b6a66b40fd5b7fadd635a41fbc6cd57db68cb` Opening a project says Building the editor (a fifth stage of the loading bar, with a spinner or its N of M line) for three drawn frames before the editor's own first frames hold the window, and Open anyway does the same (#430) *For you: When you open a project, the window now says Building the editor before it pauses to set the editor up, so you know to wait.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **settings-change-updates-its-row** `1b30041a980589dbea19e2a0e275eeadaae5d0e1` Changing a setting updates only that setting's row (its value, its Modified mark and its Reset), so the Settings list keeps its scroll position and focus and no longer rebuilds or flickers (#429) *For you: Changing a setting no longer sends the Settings list back to the top or flickers it: only the row you changed updates.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **terminal-maximise** `b8eda9a28` Shift+Cmd+J makes the terminal cover the whole window below the menu bar and again gives back its height; View > Maximise terminal and the keymap list it (#426) *For you: Press Shift+Cmd+J to make the terminal fill the window, and again to put it back.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-starts-collapsed.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-starts-collapsed.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-card-rows-carry-leds** `3fba98b20` Each row of the agent's self-test card starts with a small LED in the row's state, and the card no longer says OK, FAIL or SKIP (#421) *For you: The agent's self-test card shows a small light on each line instead of the words OK, FAIL and SKIP.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-agent-card.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-agent-card.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-led-word-same-size** `4e772646b` The agent LED's word in the terminal bar and on the self-test card is drawn at the size of the chips and rows beside it, at every text size (#422) *For you: The agent light's word is no longer bigger than the text around it.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-agent-card.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-agent-card.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-bar-says-what-each-item-is** `a8c5caf03` The terminal bar names its tabs for the program in them, shows the agent as one LED with one word, says Start agent, and gives items up in a stated order when narrow (#423) *For you: The terminal's bar is easier to read: tabs are named for what runs in them, the agent is one light with one word, and the button says Start agent.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-bar-states.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-bar-states.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-starts-collapsed** `84b5e4c5d` The terminal starts collapsed on every screen, the status bar carries a Terminal Cmd+J label that raises it, and a raise is kept across a relaunch (#424) *For you: The terminal now starts out of the way; click Terminal Cmd+J at the bottom of the window, or press the chord, to open it, and it opens the way you left it next time.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-starts-collapsed.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-terminal-starts-collapsed.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-custom-agent-needs-a-command** `c15b3e550` A saved Custom agent with no command is a settings problem that names the file and offers omp as the fix, and the Terminal agent row never offers or saves Custom without a command (#425) *For you: A terminal agent set to Custom with no command is now reported in Settings with a one-press fix, and the picker no longer lets you choose it without one.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **ui-99-gooey-review** `161fb38a` A card's picture keeps its crop and every dialog card keeps its width after the gooey swaps; the rows that wrap (library tags, cross-link buttons, review buttons, hand-back answers, a setting's actions, the kit's layer and chip rows) now put the row's own gap between their lines (UI-99, #260) *For you: The project cards' pictures and the dialogs look as they did; button rows that wrap onto a second line now leave a few more pixels between the lines.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker; the card crop, the dialog widths and the wrap gaps are proved by the_gooey_swaps_keep_what_a_person_saw*
- **ui-99-gooey-gaps** `b41c8d84` The growing columns, dialog cards, placed overlays and splash images of the editor are laid out with bevy_gooey's new layout constructors instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted, the_small_files_are_built_from_gooey_widgets and the whole editor binary*
- **ui-99-gooey-sections** `daedd101` The Map inspector's section blocks, the wish and lesson lines, the problem lines and the report note's answers are laid out with bevy_gooey's rows and columns instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted, the_small_files_are_built_from_gooey_widgets and the_agent_blocks_lines_are_gooey_rows*
- **ui-99-gooey-notes** `74c7ffd1` The notes and data editors' columns and the review thumbnail are laid out with bevy_gooey's column, row and image_box instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted and the whole editor binary*
- **ui-99-gooey-readouts** `13346151` The NightShift's readout lines, the launcher's button lines and the project page's dialog answers are laid out with bevy_gooey's rows instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted and the_small_files_are_built_from_gooey_widgets*
- **ui-99-gooey-rows** `260734cd` The library browser, the agent history, the panel heading and the dialogs' button lines are laid out with bevy_gooey's rows and columns (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted and the_small_files_are_built_from_gooey_widgets*
- **ui-99-gooey-blocks** `fb6222ae` The World, Sound and Game panels, the outliner and the inspector's blocks are laid out with bevy_gooey's rows and columns instead of hand-built nodes (UI-99, #260 part) *No clip: nobody can see this change; proved by the_editor_keeps_its_raw_nodes_counted and the_small_files_are_built_from_gooey_widgets*
- **bm-09-review-fixes** `4fbc22a2f` The benchmark report escapes a failed trial's quoted hand-back, an interrupted run writes and publishes no report, the stale-credential sweep touches only the runner's own run directories, and the docs say what the credential scrub does not cover *No clip: a runner fix; proved by a_failed_trial_quotes_render_as_text, an_interrupt_stops_the_run_cleanly, a_stale_trial_home_is_swept, a_trial_agent_inherits_no_credential and a_map_probe_matches_the_editor*
- **bm-09-benchmark-runner-hardening** `a53a76298` The benchmark runner no longer fails a task whose setup raced the editor's map (the runner waits for the map and reads a refused setup step), refuses a model name with no provider, records a tool-call budget stop (and stops only on the call beyond the budget), escapes quoted text in its report, scrubs credential strings from the kept transcript and sweeps a killed run's credential directories, and Ctrl-C stops a run through its guards *No clip: a runner fix; proved by a_refused_setup_step_is_a_harness_error, a_bare_model_name_is_refused, a_report_says_which_trials_hit_the_budget, a_report_escapes_what_it_quotes, a_transcript_holds_no_credential, a_stale_trial_home_is_swept, an_interrupt_stops_the_run_cleanly and the re-pointed a_run_measures_a_real_agent*
- **bm-08-first-real-agent-run-fixes** `c77a7842` The benchmark runner's nine defects from its first real-agent run are fixed: a trial carries one provider's credential and deletes its HOME, a dead provider is found by one pre-flight turn and stops a run after three refusals, the refusal task grades the refusal and not its wording, a tool-call budget is enforced, a report says why trials were harness errors and counts the cache tokens, a retried provider error no longer voids a trial, and the release command names --model and --jobs *No clip: a runner fix; proved by a_refusal_is_graded_on_meaning_not_wording, a_trials_credentials_do_not_outlive_it, a_dead_provider_stops_the_run, a_report_says_why_trials_were_harness_errors, a_tool_call_budget_stops_the_agent, a_reaped_agent_is_not_signalled_by_group, a_report_counts_the_cache_tokens, a_retried_provider_error_does_not_void_the_trial and tools/release/test_bench_gate.py*
- **sg-09-handback-names-style-lines** `59e6353531b1099e278c52637a432830f10b9790` A hand-back names the style lines it followed: the card lists each line's text with a link that opens it on the Design page, and the state keeps every line<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-sg-09-handback-names-style-lines.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-14-sg-09-handback-names-style-lines.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>

## 2026-11-09

- **gd-09-build-fixes** `4eb5862` The build reads cargo's real error stream, drains both its pipes so a large build cannot hang, waits for a build's last line before reporting it, saves the open map before relaunching so the game comes up on what the editor holds, and a build an agent starts is watched in the status band rather than raising a no-page problem *No clip: a fix to the watched rebuild, whose one visible change was already recorded under gd-09-build-relaunch; proved by a_build_is_watched_in_the_band_not_a_page and a_build_waits_for_the_last_line_before_it_says_what_happened and a_relaunch_reads_the_map_the_editor_holds_even_unsaved*
- **gd-09-build-relaunch** `a262da7` A change that needs Rust is a watched rebuild: BUILD in the PLAY area runs the project's build command as a background job with cargo's own count, each compiler error is a problem with its file and line, and a build that succeeds relaunches the game on the same map at the same camera while one that fails leaves the running game up *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*

- **ui-infra-review** `815d628` The staging plan stamps each panel on its own chunk's clock, and the UI budget reports a rebuild apart from a settled frame *For you: Clips of every panel no longer die on the first one, and the editor's speed check says exactly what it measures.* *No clip: a corrected clock in a tool, two faster list builds and an honest budget test, with no picture of their own; proved by tools/demo/test_stage.py (test_every_chunk_stamps_its_stage_on_its_own_start, test_a_capture_that_writes_fewer_frames_than_planned_is_refused) and bevy_dark_factory/tests/the_uis_budget_is_two_milliseconds_a_frame.rs (a_settled_frame_on_the_reference_fixture_stays_under_the_ui_budget, a_rebuild_frame_is_measured_and_is_not_claimed_as_settled, the_clock_counts_every_way_out_and_marks_a_rebuild)*
- **ui-93-budget** `5eebdf6` The UI's budget is measured on **settled frames only**: the panels that read content cost under 2 ms on a frame where nothing moved, on the issue's own fixture of 5,000 history rows and 10,000 outliner nodes. A frame that rebuilds the outliner after a change is measured and reported apart (2.9 ms for ten thousand nodes here) and is not claimed as inside the budget; the 100 ms input-to-visual figure itself is not measured, because that needs the present and the display *For you: The editor now has a number on how much work its panels do on a frame where nothing has changed, so a panel that starts rebuilding a long list is caught by a test instead of by you noticing it is slow.* *No clip: an instrument and a test, with no picture of their own; proved by bevy_dark_factory/tests/the_uis_budget_is_two_milliseconds_a_frame.rs (a_settled_frame_on_the_reference_fixture_stays_under_the_ui_budget, a_rebuild_frame_is_measured_and_is_not_claimed_as_settled, the_reference_fixture_has_the_content_the_budget_is_written_against, one_outliner_change_rebuilds_once_and_the_frames_after_it_do_not, the_clock_counts_every_way_out_and_marks_a_rebuild)*
- **ui-92-stage-panels** `0aec11c` Every panel of the editor can be staged by name for a capture, so a clip of each one starts on that panel, and `record.py` plans one frame per panel *For you: Nothing visible yet: this is the machinery a clip of each panel needs.* *No clip: an editor-side staging command and a capture plan, with no picture of its own; proved by bevy_dark_factory/tests/the_shell_can_be_staged_on_every_panel.rs (the_catalog_is_the_shells_own_panels, every_panel_in_the_catalog_stages, a_dock_panel_is_staged_by_selecting_its_tab, an_unknown_panel_is_refused_by_name, a_workbench_panel_is_refused_off_the_workbench, staging_the_workbench_leaves_the_project_open, the_staging_command_answers_with_the_arrangement_or_the_refusal) and tools/demo/test_stage.py (test_each_panel_is_its_own_chunk_of_one_frame, test_the_frame_count_the_editor_computes_is_one_per_panel, test_a_plan_with_no_panel_or_an_out_of_range_rate_is_refused, test_a_stage_step_is_its_own_kind, test_every_chunk_stamps_its_stage_on_its_own_start, test_a_capture_that_writes_fewer_frames_than_planned_is_refused)*
- **ui-91-help-pages** `8494217` Help lists every page of the guide, and each panel header carries a ? that opens the page covering what it holds *For you: Every panel now has a question mark in its header that opens the guide page about it, and the Help menu lists the whole guide.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **terminal-agent-omp-default** `c46a67bc` The terminal starts with the omp agent chosen on a machine that has it installed, and with a plain shell on one that does not; a terminal_agent in either settings file still wins (#341) *No clip: a resolved default asked of the login shell at start-up, with no picture of its own; proved by a_machine_with_the_agent_installed_starts_with_it_chosen (the_pane_offers_the_agent_on_a_machine_that_has_it_and_a_shell_on_one_that_does_not, the_settings_screen_shows_the_agent_this_machine_starts_with, the_agent_is_asked_of_the_login_shell_and_answered_there, ask_puts_the_question_to_the_shell_the_press_uses, a_path_holding_the_agent_chooses_it_and_a_path_without_it_does_not, resolving_on_this_machine_uses_what_is_installed_unless_a_file_says, the_built_in_default_is_still_the_shell)*
- **ui-57-family-panel-reads** The family settings panel reads its files only when something changed, keeps the hour it was edited to, and its read-only block follows the file it shows *For you: The settings panel no longer works in the background when nothing has changed, and the time of day you set stays where you put it.* *No clip: a fix to three behaviours inside one panel, with no picture of its own beyond the panel already recorded; proved by bevy_dark_factory/tests/a_family_panel_is_the_crates_own_settings.rs (a_settled_panel_reads_its_files_once_and_then_not_again, the_hour_a_person_set_stays_set_after_the_panel_settles, the_read_only_block_follows_its_crates_file)*
- **ui-57-family-panel** `175b7719` Each family crate the project lists gets a panel of its own settings: gooey's reflect inspector over the crate's fields, an edit written through family_set in one undo step, and the time of day moving the sky in the frame it is edited (UI-57, #243) *For you: A project's environment — time of day, date, place and climate — is now edited as fields in the Inspector, and changing the hour changes the sky at once.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **agent-guide-page** `2dd512d` The project page has an Agent guide section: the file in a text area with Save and Revert while it is allowed, Allow this guide and Trust this project while it is not, and Create guide when there is none; an edit made on disk during unsaved edits is reported and never overwritten *For you: You can now read and change the guide your agent follows without opening a text editor, and allow a project's guide from the project page.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **new-project-guide** `dd0c3a5` A new project is created with .dark_factory/AGENTS.md in it: four sections (This world, Style, Rules, Lessons) each with one line of prompt text, already allowed for the person who made it *For you: A new project comes with a guide file for agents, with the four questions worth answering already written out.* *No clip: a file in a new project; proved by a_new_project_starts_with_an_agent_guide and guide_template::tests*
- **guide-needs-trust** `e6c0c46` A project's .dark_factory/AGENTS.md reaches an agent only once a person allows that exact content (Allow this guide) or trusts the project, asked in the confirm modal with Not now remembered for the session *For you: A project you clone cannot tell your agent what to do until you have read its guide and allowed it.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **guide-two-layers** `05a727a` The agent's guide is three layers: the shipped one, the person's AGENTS.md and the open project's .dark_factory/AGENTS.md, each under a heading that names its path *For you: An agent working in DarkFactory now reads your own notes and this project's rules before it touches anything.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **af-03-lone-change** `db704ee9` A lone change is no longer paced, and a proposal becomes a fact only for its own change: an agent's undo 0.35 s after a placement frames again, and a preview followed by its apply still moves the camera once (AF-03 review round 3) *No clip: a fix to the follow's pacing and its exemption; proved by the protected the_editor_follows_an_agents_change (an_agents_undo_frames_where_the_change_was) and a_preview_is_framed_before_it_is_applied*
- **af-05-preview-outline** `ebde2d74` A preview rings its own footprint and nothing else: a piece that does not exist yet has no id, so the proposal is drawn as its rect rather than as whatever happens to stand in the box (AF-05 review round 3) *No clip: a fix to what a ring is drawn round; proved by a_preview_is_framed_before_it_is_applied (a_preview_outlines_its_own_footprint_and_not_what_stands_there)*
- **af-04-round-three** `ef557f80` The follow's queue and chip are cleared by a project change, the AGENT block has its own Look beside Undo, and the dwell between five burst changes is measured to be at least 0.6 s (AF-04 review round 3) *No clip: mostly a fix and a measurement, with no picture of their own; proved by the_person_is_never_yanked_review_two (another_project_clears_the_follows_queue, the_agent_blocks_look_button_frames_the_change, five_changes_in_one_frame_are_held_apart_by_the_dwell)*
- **af-04-look-and-pace** `bb3f91ea` The Look button works, the chip is drawn only while a change waits, a wheel or trackpad turn holds the follow, View ▸ Follow the agent writes the person's own settings layer, and the dwell paces a burst rather than an ordinary sequence (AF-04 review) *For you: The chip's Look button now really shows the change, and turning the wheel over the map counts as you using the view.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **af-05-preview** `79e59890` A preview is followed before it is applied: operation_preview and dry_run go through the same queue and idle wait, the receipt says the NightShift is about to make the change, the outline is drawn in the preview colour, a piece that does not exist yet is outlined by its footprint, and a preview followed by its apply frames once (AF-05) *For you: When your agent plans a change, the view shows the place it would change and the receipt says it is only about to happen.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **af-04-pace** `9bac13ea` The person is never yanked: the follow waits 2 s after a person works the view and shows a change 0.6 s before the next, a Look chip over the viewport names what is waiting and offers Look, and View ▸ Follow the agent (follow_agent, on by default) stops the camera moving while the chip still offers Look (AF-04) *For you: An agent's changes now wait until you stop panning or typing, then arrive one at a time; a chip over the map names what is waiting and offers Look.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **af-03-reveal-row** `1cff7e1b` A reveal lands on the row it names: the DATA table's leaf is scrolled to its own row, and the rig, data, settings and note rows are proved on screen (AF-03 review) *No clip: a fix to a reveal's measurement, with no picture of its own; proved by the_reveal_selects_a_row_a_person_can_see (7 tests)*
- **af-03-reveal** `1ca509d2` An agent's change off the map is followed too: the editor opens the Kit, Rigs or Map door on the panel the change is on, selects the piece, group, rig, file or note, and scrolls its row into view; a change with no page is a problem naming it (AF-03) *For you: When your agent changes a piece, a tile, a group, a rig, a tuning file or a note, the editor now opens that page with the thing it changed selected.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **agent-wish-guide** `e8853270` The shipped MCP guide gains an After a task: reflect section: after the hand-back an agent makes one blueprint_wish, says what a good wish is and what one is not, and the notes agent's prompt names the tool *No clip: shipped guide prose and two prompt constants, with no picture of their own; proved by dark_factory_mcp/tests/the_guide_asks_for_a_wish.rs (the_guide_asks_for_a_wish_and_names_every_field, the_reflect_section_says_what_a_wish_is_not, the_reflect_sections_example_is_a_wish_the_editor_takes, the_shipped_reference_carries_the_reflect_section) and bevy_dark_factory/tests/the_prompts_ask_for_a_wish.rs (the_notes_agents_prompt_asks_for_a_wish_after_the_handback, the_self_test_prompt_makes_no_wish)*
- **af-02-follow** `c1b18203` The editor frames an agent's map change: the camera eases onto where it is, the changed pieces are ringed, the view opens the Map first, and the receipt names it the NightShift's; an agent's undo frames where the change was (AF-02) *For you: When your agent changes the map, the view goes to what it changed instead of staying where you left it.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-af-02-follow.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-af-02-follow.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **fleet-launcher** `381ecdb6` A fleet launcher opens one DeepSeek omp worker per issue in herdr, on any host *No clip: loop tooling; proved by launching this run's five workers*
- **agent-wish** `80cde67` After a hand-back, blueprint_wish records the one tool that would most have helped, one per hand-back, queued in the editor and filed through a new agent-wish issue form *No clip: an MCP tool and an issue address, with no picture of their own; proved by a_wish_is_refused_at_the_door (3), a_wish_issue_address_round_trips (3), an_agent_wishes_for_one_tool (6) and docs_are_current (6)*
- **mcp-guide** `a39ecd68` The MCP server ships a guide to working in DarkFactory and serves it as blueprint_guide; the server's instructions and the notes agent's prompt both say to call it first *For you: An agent working in DarkFactory now reads a guide first, in an order that matches how a task actually goes.* *No clip: an MCP tool and its shipped text; proved by dark_factory_mcp/tests/the_guide_is_the_servers_own.rs (the_guide_is_a_read_that_needs_no_editor, the_instructions_name_the_guide_first, the_guide_names_every_tool_the_server_declares) and bevy_dark_factory/tests/the_guides_examples_run.rs (the_guides_example_runs_against_the_editors_operations, an_example_naming_an_operation_that_does_not_exist_is_caught)*
- **af-01-shown** `254f29ae` Every operation says where its change is seen: blueprint_apply_operation, preview and undo answer shown — a box on the map, a piece, a composition, a rig, a note, a project file or a setting (AF-01) *No clip: an API: a new required method on Operation and a new field in three answers; proved by an_operation_says_where_its_change_is_seen (every_operation_says_where_its_change_is_seen, a_change_names_a_box_that_holds_the_placement, a_preview_and_its_apply_name_the_same_place)*
- **json-schema-marks-only-where-they-belong** `80b9e20b` A schema marks a field only where it names another file's value, and nothing in the crate exists for a test's sake (#366) *No clip: generated schema marks; proved by tests/schema_describes_every_file_kind.rs*
- **json-schema-followups** `939602b9` A schema file is found by the reload table where it is written, every field that names a piece, composition, rig, placement or map carries its kind, and the schema hint is relative to the YAML file that carries it (GD-04, #366) *For you: Editors outside the app now find each file's schema, and following a field that names a piece or a rig goes to the right file.* *No clip: a reload row, generated schemas and a hint line; proved by tests/schema_describes_every_file_kind.rs*
- **json-schema-per-kind** `8f7bfc85` Every project file kind publishes a JSON Schema generated from its type, with a field's doc comment as its description and x-dark-factory-ref on a field that names another file's value; blueprint_schema returns one (GD-04, #366) *For you: An editor outside the app now knows what each project file may say, so it completes fields and explains them.* *No clip: generated files and a read tool; proved by tests/schema_describes_every_file_kind.rs*
- **yaml-surgery** `f5f64591` One YAML value is edited in place, and every comment, blank line and key order survives; an edit of another kind or outside its bounds is refused, naming the leaf (GD-02, #364) *For you: A change made by an agent or by you now rewrites only the value it changes, so your comments in a project file stay where you wrote them.* *No clip: a library; proved by tests/yaml_surgery_keeps_every_comment.rs (14 tests, line diffs)*
- **yaml-reader-alias-and-first-fault** `9f771901` A YAML file's alias is refused as an alias and the first fault in the file is the one reported; the round trip proves against the frozen RON fixtures (GD-01, #363) *For you: When a project file is wrong in more than one place, the editor now names the first one, and it says an alias is an alias.* *No clip: a reader's refusals and its test fixtures; proved by tests/yaml_refuses_a_file_it_cannot_read.rs and tests/yaml_round_trips_every_project_type.rs*
- **yaml-reader** `61bbad3a` One YAML reader for project files, refusing an anchor, an alias, a merge key, a duplicate key, a second document and a tab indentation, each with its line and field path (GD-01, #363) *For you: Project files can now be read and written as YAML, and a file that is ambiguous is refused with the line and field it is wrong at.* *No clip: a reader and a writer, engine-free; proved by tests/yaml_refuses_a_file_it_cannot_read.rs and tests/yaml_round_trips_every_project_type.rs*
- **ui-80-view-light-map** `67e5831` View ▸ Light map shows and hides the viewport overlay and its tick follows the overlay whichever door changed it; the overlay's two colours read the `overlay_dark` and `overlay_lit` tones of whatever skin is running, so a project's `family/bevy_gooey.ron` that defines them moves the overlay, while the shipped skins — the editor's own, Tool dark, Parchment and High contrast — carry neither tone and draw the pair it always did (UI-80, #254) *For you: There is now a Light map item in the View menu, and a project can choose the two colours the light map is drawn in.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **ui-73-theme-and-text-size** `e1e33e3` The Theme and Text size settings reach the running skin: Tool dark and High contrast are gooey skins, applied; the text size is the skin's text_scale (UI-73, #251) *For you: You can pick High contrast in Settings now. Parchment is listed but cannot be shown yet, because its pale panels leave the editor's own labels unreadable, so choosing it keeps the look you had and says why. A larger text size enlarges the interface built on the new library; the editor's older labels do not grow with it yet.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: fleet worker*
- **ui-02-agent-tab-ink** `9768b89` The agent tab's self-test card and status chip draw in the running skin's ink, so a project's bevy_gooey skin reaches the terminal too (UI-02, #210) *No clip: a seam with no change to the eye: the editor's own skin carries these four colours, so the card looks identical today; proved by tests/the_agent_tabs_ink_comes_from_the_skin.rs, whose card-and-chip test fails when the repaint is removed*
- **terminal-chips-explain** `0f671f50` Every chip in the terminal's bar says what it does when the pointer rests on it (the agent's self-test, a session, Agent, +, x); the move chip reads Go to <folder> instead of cd <folder>, with the command it types in its tooltip, and an agent's tab is no longer offered it (feedback-1794230949329) *For you: The little buttons in the terminal's top bar now explain themselves when you point at them, and the confusing 'cd' button no longer shows up on an agent.* *No clip: tooltips and a label on a bar's chips; proved by tests/capture_feedback_1794230949329.rs, and a real editor under Xvfb showing each tooltip*
- **ui-56-data-table** `266f6dc7` The DATA panel is a sortable table of file, path and value; opening a row puts a text field over its value, and Enter writes one data_set (UI-56). *For you: The game data panel is now a table you can sort, and a value is changed in place.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-56-data-table.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-56-data-table.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-55-note-body** `d4b37291` A note is edited on several lines in the Notes panel: Edit opens a text area, Enter adds a line, Ctrl+Enter saves it as one note_edit (UI-55). *For you: You can now edit a note's text in the Notes panel, on as many lines as you like.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-55-note-body.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-55-note-body.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-54-problems** `b2e28b5c` The Map inspector's PROBLEMS section lists each broken relation in the RELATIONS row's words, and Go to selects the pieces the relation names, the one it is about first, and frames them (UI-54). *For you: A new PROBLEMS list names every rule the map breaks, and Go to takes you to the pieces involved.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-54-problems.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-54-problems.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-60b-plan** `ae32bfa4` The AGENT block lists the agent's plan: a PLAN row under HAND-BACK with the count (plan: 1 of 3 done) and each step with its status, read from the plan_set file twice a second, so a step the agent marks done is ticked there (UI-60b). *For you: The agent's block now shows the plan the agent made, and ticks each step as it is done.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-60b-plan.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-ui-60b-plan.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **empty-map-clear** `5928ac32` A project with no pieces no longer puts an Add meshes… button over the map: the map is left clear and the PLACE list in the right panel is the one place that offers it (feedback-1794147258746, feedback-1794230462401) *For you: A new project's map is no longer covered by an Add meshes button: it is in the right panel, where the pieces go.* *No clip: a control removed from the map's overlay; proved by tests/capture_feedback_1794230462401.rs*
- **gooey-0-52** `2c4dd910` The editor's UI library moves from bevy_gooey 0.43.1 to 0.52.1: the LED widget, a tooltip whose keycap no longer flickers between two widths (bevy_gooey#79), a popover that opens from its anchor, a collapsed panel that peeks, and the fix this move needed (a split's panes were marked as interface and took every click meant for the map) *No clip: a dependency pin: the changes it carries are the library's own; proved by the editor's whole suite (1982 tests) and bevy_gooey's tests/split_pane_hover.rs*
- **agent-led** `c59ffe49` The agent has an LED: in the terminal's bar for as long as an agent is chosen (a hollow ring that says off until you invoke one, then a pulsing amber square while it checks, a green disc when it is live, a red diamond when a check failed, each with its word beside it), and ending the self-test card; the bar's LED stays lit after the card has gone (feedback-1794230412409) *For you: When you run an agent in DarkFactory's terminal, a small light now shows whether it is live: dark before you start it, amber while it checks, green once it is connected, red if its self test failed.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-agent-led.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-09-agent-led.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>

## 2026-11-08

- **agent-resumes-after-quit** `e451b86d` Close DarkFactory with omp running and it is back where it was: DarkFactory's omp extension reports the session id (blueprint/agent_session), the editor keeps one record per project, and the next time the project is open with omp chosen the agent's tab opens by itself and types omp --resume <id>, with no self-test prompt; closing the tab or leaving omp clears it (feedback-1794148425925) *For you: If DarkFactory is closed while your agent is running, reopening the project brings the agent back in the same conversation.* *No clip: an agent tab opening by itself and the line it types, with no picture of its own; proved by capture_feedback_1794148425925 (the line a second editor types is omp --resume with the reported session, and a closed, left or silent agent is not resumed) and a real omp run against a stand-in BRP server that posted its session id and null on shutdown*
- **refusal-messages-without-blank-runs** `af13efd1` A refusal shown on the screen no longer breaks in the middle: thirteen messages (the map_create and map_set_settings refusals an agent causes, the vocab, composition and labelling messages, a glb reader error) had a run of 14 to 34 blanks where a line continuation had been collapsed, so a toast wrapped them into two ragged lines (feedback-1794147495932) *For you: A refusal message that an agent's mistake puts on the screen now reads as one sentence, not as two ragged lines with a gap in the middle.* *No clip: message text with no picture beyond a wrapped toast; proved by capture_feedback_1794147495932 (six refusals an agent causes read back from the status banner hold no run of blanks) and ops::tests::the_refusal_for_a_maps_parameters_is_one_unbroken_sentence*
- **wheel-belongs-to-the-map-only-over-the-map** `731b7677` A wheel turned over the terminal, the menu bar or the status band no longer zooms the map: it zooms only with the pointer inside the map's own viewport (feedback-1794147411369) *For you: Scrolling in the terminal (or over the menu bar or status bar) no longer zooms the map; the wheel zooms the map only when the pointer is over the map.* *No clip: input routing with no picture of its own beyond the map's zoom; proved by capture_feedback_1794147411369 (a wheel notch at every point of the chrome and over the terminal pane leaves the rig's zoom, and one inside the viewport still zooms) and view::tests::the_wheel_is_the_maps_only_inside_the_viewport*
- **terminal-mac-line-editing** `80588400` In the terminal on a Mac, Cmd+Backspace clears the line the shell is reading back to its start (^U), and Cmd+Left and Cmd+Right go to its start and end (^A, ^E), as in VS Code's and iTerm2's terminals; every other Command chord is still never the shell's (feedback-1794146958145) *For you: In the terminal, Cmd+Backspace now clears the line you are typing, and Cmd+Left and Cmd+Right jump to its start and end.* *No clip: keys into a shell with no picture of their own; proved by capture_feedback_1794146958145 (cat -v is sent the line after Cmd+Backspace, aa then ok, as ok and not aaok) and terminal_view::tests::command_backspace_left_and_right_send_the_line_editing_codes*
- **left-panel-and-zen-mode** `7545878d` Cmd+B (Ctrl+B elsewhere) shows and hides the left panel and the viewport takes the room; Shift+Cmd+M (Shift+Ctrl+M) is Zen mode, which folds the menu bar, toolbar, docks, terminal and status band away until the same chord is pressed again, with a toast to say so; View, Show left panel and View, Zen mode are the same two as ticks; the run tool moves from Cmd+B to Shift+Cmd+B (feedback-1794146831022) *For you: Cmd+B hides and shows the left panel, and Shift+Cmd+M hides everything but the map until you press it again. (Run is Shift+Cmd+B now.)* *No clip: a layout change a chord toggles, with no clip rendered for it; proved by capture_feedback_1794146831022 (the left dock and every Zen part lay out to nothing and the viewport takes the room, then both come back)*
- **empty-prompt-yields-to-an-agent** `45a46830` The empty map's prompt (Place a piece from the library, Open library) goes while an agent is invoked, since an agent working the map is what to do next and its forest is not a placement, and comes back when the agent's tab is closed (feedback-1794146746836) *For you: When you invoke an agent, the 'Place a piece from the library' hint on an empty map steps aside.* *No clip: an overlay that is a spawn-or-despawn decision over a viewport; proved by capture_feedback_1794146746836 and empty_state::tests::the_prompt_stands_only_on_an_empty_map_with_no_question_and_no_agent*
- **terminal-exit-closes-its-tab** `ddeeebec` A terminal whose shell was told to exit (exit, Ctrl+D, or leaving omp and then exit) closes its tab instead of staying up over a dead shell; the pane is then the Invoke Agent button (or a fresh shell with no agent chosen) and + opens another; a shell that ends before anything was typed to it keeps its tab with the reason (feedback-1794112080244) *For you: Typing exit in the terminal now closes it, and you can open another, instead of leaving a frozen terminal behind.* *No clip: the pane's behaviour after a shell leaves, with no picture of its own to show; proved by capture_feedback_1794112080244 and terminal::tests::a_shell_told_to_exit_takes_its_tab_with_it*
- **chords-in-words** `edb51e56` The editor spells a chord in words, so no keycap draws a glyph its face lacks: the Undo tooltip reads Cmd+Z where it drew a box with a question mark (feedback-1794097488318, and feedback-1793927744511 before it); gooey's ChordGlyphs::Words reaches every tooltip, menu row, badge and palette row, and on Windows and Linux the words are what they were *For you: Keyboard shortcuts in tooltips and menus now read Cmd+Z instead of a symbol the editor's font could not draw, which showed as a question mark.* *No clip: a spelling on a keycap that shows only while a pointer rests on a button; proved by capture_feedback_1794097488318 and ui::tests::every_chord_spelled_on_a_mac_is_drawable_in_the_face_the_editor_ships*
- **check-for-updates-control** `cadd7a05` The launcher has a quiet Check for updates button under its version line, to look again for a release published after the app opened; it only looks (never downloads), says Checking for updates… while it does, and an Update to v… button stays above it (feedback-1794089325724) *No clip: a small control on the launcher's sidebar, in the macOS app only (the updater exists only in a .app), so the headless recorder cannot draw it; proved by tests/capture_feedback_1794089325724.rs and the unit tests in update.rs*
- **agent-card-one-line-rows** `2c1d8606` The agent self-test card takes the width its longest row needs, so each row is one line: 'OK Agent connected: omp-coding-agent 1.0.0 · 22 tools' no longer breaks with 'tools' alone under the mark (feedback-1794089256357) *No clip: a width rule on a card that shows for seconds after Invoke Agent; proved by tests/capture_feedback_1794089256357.rs and the unit test in terminal_pane.rs*
- **terminal-one-door-to-the-agent** `a156f007` The terminal's bar no longer draws an Agent chip beside the empty pane's Invoke Agent button: with an agent chosen and nothing open the button is the one way in, and the chip returns once a tab is open, to add another (feedback-1794088663844) *No clip: visible only with an agent chosen in the settings, where the empty terminal loses a duplicate control; proved by tests/capture_feedback_1794088663844.rs and the unit test in terminal_pane.rs*
- **ui-60b-hand-back** `0a13b061` The agent's hand-back is answered in the AGENT block when Answer hand-backs here is on: a card under the HAND-BACK line says what was made, what you said, what the agent assumed and where to look, with Accept, Undo N operations and Dismiss; the modal stays the default, and a block closed on a waiting card hands it to the modal (UI-60b, #340 slice) *For you: With Answer hand-backs here on, the agent's hand-back is answered in the AGENT block instead of a dialog.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-60b-hand-back.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-60b-hand-back.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-r06-tool-light** `22f4c2e2` The toolbar's tool light slides from the armed tool to the new one instead of changing in place, on gooey's springs (G-61, bevy_gooey 0.43.1); hidden at rest, lands within half a pixel, turns round without a jump, and lands at once with Reduced motion (UI-R06, #336 slice) *For you: The toolbar's tool light now slides to the tool you pick.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-r06-tool-light.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-r06-tool-light.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-60-agent-block** `3ec9af68` View > Show agent: an AGENT block in the INSPECTOR says when the agent was last heard, whose the newest change is and what it was, and what it handed back; its Undo takes the agent's change back (greyed unless the newest change is the agent's), and its switch is the View row's twin (UI-60, #246) *For you: View > Show agent shows what the agent did and lets you undo it in one press.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-60-agent-block.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-60-agent-block.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **fix-project-agent-invite** `e874a0ab` A project that chooses the terminal's agent in its own editor.ron now gets the empty pane with Invoke Agent, as the user's settings do: the first shell waits for the project and its settings file instead of opening two frames before them (#339) *No clip: a bug fix to the pane the 2026-11-08 Invoke Agent clip already shows; the gap is two frames wide at start-up, so no headless test can show it; proved by a probe of a real editor under Xvfb (an Invoke Agent button at 18 s and 58 s where a shell had been)*
- **ui-93-outliner-budget** `893349be` The outliner does no work on a settled frame: the tree is rebuilt only when the map, the library or the search moved, and is a screenful of entities however many pieces there are (UI-93, #259) *No clip: no visible change: a per-frame cost removed; proved by tests/the_outliner_does_no_work_on_a_settled_frame.rs*
- **ui-60-agent-pause** `893349be` View > Pause agent writes: while it is on, the agent's operation_apply and operation_undo are refused with the reason, on the status line as a problem and in the answer, and nothing changes or is logged; previews still answer and a person's own edits are untouched (UI-60, #246) *For you: View > Pause agent writes stops the agent changing your map until you turn it off.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-60-agent-pause.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-60-agent-pause.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-52-piece-drop** `7d876dfc` A library piece dragged from the PLACE list and let go over the map is placed where the pointer is, snapped like a click and undone in one step; the viewport lights as the place it may land (UI-52, #238) *For you: Drag a piece from the Place list onto the map to put it down where you let go.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-52-piece-drop.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-52-piece-drop.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-51-outliner** `10d4addb` View > Show outliner puts an OUTLINER section at the top of the INSPECTOR that lists the map's pieces in a tree grouped by what they are; a search narrows it to the matching pieces and their groups, and a row selects its piece in the viewport (UI-51, #237) *For you: View > Show outliner lists every piece on the map in the Inspector: search for a name, click it, and the piece is selected.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-51-outliner.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-ui-51-outliner.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>

## 2026-11-07

- **ui-91-shortcuts** `090f9f0b` Help > Keyboard shortcuts is generated from the command registry: one row per command on the chord it answers to now, so a command added tomorrow is on it and a rebinding (even one made in the settings file) shows at once (UI-91, #257) *For you: Help > Keyboard shortcuts lists every command with the key it answers to, including the ones you changed.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-91-shortcuts.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-91-shortcuts.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-50-inspector** `467e4767` The inspector shows the selected pieces' pose in gooey's reflect inspector: with several pieces selected a field they disagree on reads a dash, and typing a facing or a lift turns or lifts all of them (each keeping its own tip) in one undo step; the pieces on screen follow the number (UI-50, #236) *For you: Select several pieces with Shift-click and the inspector shows what they share: type a facing once and every one of them turns.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-50-inspector.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-50-inspector.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-r04-conventions** `302f5e00` The launcher takes gooey's desktop conventions: typing a project's first letters moves to its card (TypeSelectGroup, G-68), a card shrinks under the press (PressScale 0.97), a tooltip waits 0.5 s, a double press opens a card (UI-R04, #334)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-r03-r05-launcher.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-r03-r05-launcher.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-r03-buttons** `3466179d` The editor's buttons say what they do: AccentButton is gone for gooey's Submit variant, toolbar icon buttons are Ghost, a destructive confirmation's agreeing answer is Danger, and Return in the New project form presses Create (UI-R03, #333)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-r03-dialog.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-r03-dialog.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-r05-scroll-fade** `55d10371` Every scroll list the editor draws through chrome::scroll_list glides on programmatic scrolls and fades at the edge that hides rows; the launcher's project list shows a bottom fade while rows are hidden and none at its end (UI-R05, #335)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-r03-r05-launcher.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-ui-r03-r05-launcher.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **invoke-agent-enter** `5955d4c3` Enter presses Invoke Agent while the terminal has the keyboard, so a keyboard-only person can still start the agent *No clip: a key, not a picture; proved by tests/the_terminal_starts_no_agent_until_it_is_invoked.rs (enter_starts_the_agent_for_a_person_at_the_keyboard_and_only_when_the_terminal_has_the_keys)*
- **invoke-agent** `607bce98` The terminal starts no agent by itself: with an agent chosen it opens empty with an Invoke Agent button in the middle, and only the press starts the session (no tokens are spent just looking around)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-invoke-agent.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-08-invoke-agent.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **338** `b5371788` The release smoke launches a fresh app when its window never took the foreground, so a release started while someone is using the Mac is not refused for it *No clip: release tooling, not drawn; proved by tools/release/macos/test_smoke_app.py*
- **337** `f766fb33` Updating in place no longer freezes the app or strands the swap: a closing terminal, the swap script and a quit that does not finish are each bounded *For you: Updating DarkFactory from inside the app no longer leaves it frozen: if the old app is slow to quit, the update ends it after a few seconds and opens the new version by itself.* *No clip: a fix to the update path and the app's exit, not drawn; proved by closing_a_terminal_never_waits_on_its_child, an_update_swaps_even_when_the_old_app_will_not_leave and a_quit_that_does_not_finish_is_ended*
- **324-dark_factory-project** `ec36b43e` the terminal: every shell is told the open project in DARK_FACTORY_PROJECT (#324) *No clip: an environment variable; no screen changes; proved by the_shell_knows_the_project_it_runs_beside*
- **325-terminal-links** `cf6a8b42` the terminal: Cmd+click (Ctrl+click elsewhere) on an http:// or https:// address in the output opens it (#325) *For you: Hold Cmd (Ctrl off a Mac) and click an address a build or git printed in the terminal, and it opens in your browser.* *No clip: the click opens the system's browser, a window outside the editor, so the editor's picture shows no change; proved by a_link_in_the_terminal_opens_on_a_primary_click*
- **UI-R02-depth** `0b9ef016` The editor's skin states its shadow stacks: panels, menus and dialogs stand at three heights from the skin file *For you: The editor's panels, menus and dialogs stand at three heights, and the skin file now holds those shadows so they can be tuned.* *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: no display on big; the depth itself arrived with the bevy_gooey pin of UI-R01, so this line is the skin stating it; proved by tests/the_editor_skin_has_depth.rs*
- **UI-R01-motion** `541d7875` The editor's dialogs fade out as well as in, colours ease to the skin's, and Reduced motion is bevy_gooey's: the command palette, settings and keymap leave on a spring, and the chosen tab fades with its dialog *For you: Dialogs now fade out instead of vanishing, colours ease when a theme or a hover changes, and the Reduced motion setting keeps fades while it stops things sliding.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-331-palette-leaves.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-07-331-palette-leaves.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-shell-setting** `37dba1a9` the terminal: the shell and its arguments are settings (terminal_shell, terminal_shell_args) (#324) *For you: Choose the program a new terminal tab runs, and its arguments, in settings.ron or a project's editor.ron, the way VS Code's default terminal profile does.* *No clip: a settings key with no settings-screen row; the tab looks as any shell does; proved by the_terminal_runs_the_shell_it_is_set_to::a_tab_runs_the_shell_the_settings_name_with_its_arguments*
- **279-stepped-clock** `2f89a1da` The family settings reload test steps the editor's clock instead of sleeping, so the push gate no longer fails on a loaded machine *For you: The push gate no longer refuses a change because the machine was busy.* *No clip: test-only: no screen changes; proved by family_settings_reload::an_edited_settings_file_reaches_the_editor_and_a_bad_one_is_a_problem*
- **227-saved-shell-layout** `b550459f` The shell's arrangement is saved per person, and View ▸ Reset layout puts the default back *For you: Panels you move stay where you put them after a restart, and View > Reset layout puts the editor's default arrangement back.* *No clip: no visible change on screen at a settled editor; proved by a_saved_arrangement_is_the_one_the_editor_shows*
- **305-toast-below-bar** `f4697da2` A toast stands under the menu bar instead of over its titles *For you: A toast no longer covers the menu bar: the tour offer and every refusal now appear just below it.* *No clip: a toast moved 28 px down; proved by a_toast_never_stands_over_the_menu_bar and chrome::keep_toasts_under_the_menu_bar*
- **feedback-1794062374181** The terminal draws the gap a tab leaves as blank columns, not a missing-glyph box after every name `ls` lines up *For you: In the terminal, ls and other column output no longer shows a "?" box in the gaps between names.* *No clip: a tab's cell drawn as a space, the same columns as before; proved by capture_feedback_1794062374181 and terminal::grid::tests*
