- **release-0.3.4** `9ea50db` DarkFactory 0.3.4: a click puts the keyboard in the terminal, and Ctrl+D is the shell's *For you: A new build where clicking the terminal lets you type into it at once, and Ctrl+D ends a program in the shell instead of opening a bug report.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the changes it carries have their own lines above*
- **terminal-ctrl-chords** `561cf1c` Ctrl+D in the terminal ends what is running there instead of also opening a report *For you: In the terminal, Ctrl+D ends a program or the shell as it should, without a report window popping up over it.* *No clip: keyboard routing, nothing drawn; proved by the_shell_keeps_its_ctrl_chords*
- **terminal-click-focus** `d89a8d5` A click on the terminal puts the keyboard in the shell, and a click elsewhere gives it back *For you: Click the terminal to type into it; click anywhere else to give the keys back to the screen.* *No clip: keyboard focus, nothing new is drawn; proved by a_click_puts_the_keyboard_in_the_terminal*
- **release-0.3.3** DarkFactory 0.3.3: the terminal is on every screen, and Cmd+J or Ctrl+J tucks it away *For you: A new build where the terminal is open under every screen from the start; Cmd+J on a Mac, or Ctrl+J elsewhere, collapses it and brings it back.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the changes it carries have their own lines above, and the terminal's clip is terminal-every-screen*
- **terminal-holds-the-keys** `dd66a11` While the terminal has the keyboard, the screen's own navigation no longer acts on the keys you type *For you: Typing arrows, Tab and Enter into the terminal no longer moves around or presses buttons on the screen behind it.* *No clip: keyboard routing, nothing drawn; proved by the_terminal_is_on_every_screen::the_home_does_not_act_on_keys_the_shell_has*
- **terminal-collapse-chord** `dd66a11` Cmd+J (macOS) or Ctrl+J collapses the terminal and brings it back, on every screen *For you: Press Cmd+J on a Mac, or Ctrl+J elsewhere, to tuck the terminal away; press it again to bring it back with the keys in the shell.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-every-screen.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-every-screen.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-every-screen** `dd66a11` The terminal is open on every screen from the moment DarkFactory starts, docked across the bottom *For you: The terminal is now always there, under the map, the project page and the projects home, with the same shell and scrollback on every screen.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-every-screen.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-every-screen.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **release-0.3.2** DarkFactory 0.3.2: full-screen programs get their keys and mouse in the terminal *For you: A new build whose terminal lets you scroll less and man with the wheel, click in htop or vim, and use arrow keys in every full-screen program.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the changes it carries have their own lines above, and the terminal's clip is terminal-wheel-programs*
- **terminal-wheel-programs** `3151855` The mouse wheel scrolls less, man and git log, and a program that asks for the mouse gets its clicks *For you: Scroll less, man or git log with the wheel in the terminal, and click in htop, vim or lazygit; hold Shift to select text instead.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-programs.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-programs.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-cursor-keys** `3151855` Arrow, Home and End keys reach a full-screen program in the mode it asked for, and Ctrl+arrows jump by word *For you: Arrow keys work in htop, tig and other full-screen programs in the terminal, and Ctrl with an arrow moves a word at a time in your shell.* *No clip: keystroke bytes, not drawn; proved by the_terminal_speaks_to_programs_in_their_modes::an_arrow_is_ss3_in_application_cursor_mode and terminal::input_tests*
- **release-0.3.1** DarkFactory 0.3.1: the terminal draws what programs ask and keeps the prompt in view *For you: A new build whose terminal shows inverse text, coloured backgrounds and your selection, and keeps the prompt on screen after a long command.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the changes it carries have their own lines above, and the terminal's clip is terminal-grounds*
- **terminal-rows-fit** `1610145` The terminal's rows are one line tall at the editor's scale, so the prompt stays in the pane after a screenful of output *For you: After a long command the terminal's prompt stays visible at the bottom of the pane, and the caret sits where you are typing.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-grounds.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-grounds.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **terminal-grounds** `b9877f2` The terminal draws inverse text, coloured backgrounds and the selection, instead of leaving them invisible *For you: Inverse text (less's status line, a TUI's highlighted row), coloured backgrounds and your drag selection now show in the terminal.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-grounds.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-06-terminal-grounds.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **feedback-1793968919654** `c893709` Kit packs on the Kits door start collapsed; only the pack holding the selected mesh is open *No clip: a default state of an existing list; proved by capture_feedback_1793968919654*
- **terminal-output-path** `f89e1df` The terminal parses a shell's output in place and draws nothing on a frame where the screen did not change *No clip: a performance change on the terminal's output path; 1000 one-byte feeds 35 ms -> 21 us measured on big; proved by terminal::flow_tests (a_screen_is_restamped_on_a_change_and_only_then, a_flood_never_holds_one_drain, ctrl_c_stops_a_flood_and_the_shell_goes_quiet)*
- **feedback-1793928006124** The strip's cue to the other door reads 'Switch to Map' instead of a bare 'Map' that looked like a fourth tab *For you: The door cue beside the tabs now says "Switch to Map" (or Kit), so it no longer looks like another tab.* *No clip: a one-word label change; proved by capture_feedback_1793928006124_switch*
- **feedback-1793927950267-rail** `f89e1df` Choosing a sidebar tab on the home moves the selection rail with it, instead of leaving the old tab railed too *No clip: a one-frame visual in the launcher sidebar; proved by capture_feedback_1793927950267_rail*
- **release-0.3.0** DarkFactory 0.3.0: Fill sits with the tools it belongs to *For you: A new build with Fill the same size and shape as Place and Move, instead of standing a head taller than the undo and save icons it sat between.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the change it carries has its own line above, and its clip is that line's*
- **capture-1793927744511** A chord glyph the font cannot draw is filed with the test that names it *No clip: no visible change yet: the Mac spelling is bevy_gooey's choice (bevy-coven/bevy_gooey#45) and this repo must not patch or vendor it; proved by the_installed_face_covers_every_chord_glyph, which reads the installed font's cmap and names the six glyphs it lacks*
- **capture-1793927698061** Fill stands beside the other named tools instead of among the bare icons *For you: Fill is the same height and shape as Place and Move now, instead of standing a head taller than the undo and save icons it sat between.* *No clip: a one-line spawn order in the toolbar; proved by a_labelled_button_is_never_the_odd_one_in_a_run_of_pictures, which prints Fill at 46.7 logical px among 30.8 px pictures before the change and a uniform run after*
- **capture-1793927671762** A prompt keycap is filed with the failing test that proves its letter is not centred *No clip: no visible change — the cause is in bevy_gooey (bevy-coven/bevy_gooey#44) and this repo must not patch or vendor it; proved by the_remove_mode_keycap_centres_its_letter, which measures -5.0 logical px off centre on the current pin and is ignored until the fix lands*

## 2026-11-05

- **release-0.2.9** DarkFactory 0.2.9: the terminal docked where it belongs *For you: A new build with the terminal across the bottom of the window, with the map still usable above it.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the change it carries has its own line above*
- **fix-0.2.9-terminal-pane** The terminal docked across the bottom of the window instead of over the map *For you: Press Cmd+` and the terminal opens across the bottom of the window with the map still usable above it, instead of being laid out off the right edge.* *No clip: a layout one-liner; proved by the_pane_spans_the_window_and_sits_above_the_status_band, which fails on the 0.2.8 parenting and passes on this one*
- **release-0.2.8** DarkFactory 0.2.8: a terminal in the editor *For you: A new build with a terminal docked across the bottom of the window: press Cmd+` and run cargo, git or your agent without leaving DarkFactory.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the change it carries has its own line above, and its clip is that line's*
- **terminal-in-the-editor** Ctrl+` opens a real shell in the editor's own window, docked across the bottom *For you: Press Cmd+` and a terminal opens at the bottom of the window, already in your project: run cargo, git or your agent right there instead of switching apps.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-05-terminal-in-the-editor.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-05-terminal-in-the-editor.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **release-0.2.7** `0.2.7` DarkFactory 0.2.7: the five filed captures answered *For you: A new build with the What's new page scrolling and readable, a sidebar with its margin, no flash when a project opens, and a download bar on Update.* *No clip: a version bump and nothing else; the changes it carries each have their own line above*
- **news-prose-check** A decision model can be asked whether the What's new page's sentences read as English, and the runner now hands a check its facts *For you: Nothing changes on screen; this is the tooling that lets a model judge the words the page shows.* *No clip: a tool that asks a model about text, not a visible change; proved by tools/decide/test_news_prose.py and a live run whose facts the checks.ron reads*
- **feedback-1793903311065** Update downloads behind a bar, and the button comes back when it finishes *For you: Pressing Update shows a bar that fills as the download comes down, with the percentage on the button, and the button comes back when it is done.* *No clip: a download the harness cannot run (macOS-only) so the visible part is proven headless against the state a real download writes; proved by an_update_downloads_behind_a_bar (a_download_draws_a_bar_that_fills_to_its_length, the_bar_gives_way_to_the_button_when_the_download_is_done, a_download_that_states_no_length_draws_an_empty_bar_rather_than_a_wrong_one)*
- **feedback-1793901499397** The project page's first frame has its sidebar, so the garbled flash is gone *For you: Opening a project no longer flashes a page with no sidebar: the first frame you see is already the whole page.* *No clip: a one-frame layout defect, and no display on this host while another tenant holds the GPU; proved by capture_feedback_1793901499397 (the_pages_first_frame_has_a_sidebar, no_frame_of_the_page_draws_the_body_without_the_sidebar)*
- **feedback-1793905698793** The What's new page's prose is set at a readable measure instead of the window's width *For you: The change notes on What's new are set in a narrower column now, so a line is something you can read without losing your place.* *No clip: a measure change on one page, and no display on this host while another tenant holds the GPU; proved by capture_feedback_1793905698793 (the_whats_new_pages_prose_fits_a_readable_measure, the_prose_column_does_not_grow_with_the_window)*
- **feedback-1793905798698** The sidebar's splash keeps the same margin on every side *For you: The splash in the sidebar no longer runs out of its right edge: it has the same margin on every side now.* *No clip: no display on this host while another tenant holds the GPU; proved by capture_feedback_1793905798698 (the_splash_has_the_same_margin_on_every_side, the_splash_shrinks_with_the_sidebar_rather_than_hanging_out_of_it)*
- **feedback-1793905912313** The What's new page scrolls, so its last entries and See every change are reachable *For you: The What's new page scrolls now: the entries below the fold and the See every change button come into view instead of being cut off.* *No clip: no display on this host while another tenant holds the GPU; proved by capture_feedback_1793905912313 (the_whats_new_page_can_be_reached_to_its_bottom, the_whats_new_page_scrolls_with_the_wheel_over_it)*
- **project-picture-cache** The home and the project page share one decoded picture per project instead of decoding it again for every card, body and redraw *No clip: no visible change: the frame a project's page first draws is a picture the home already uploaded, measured identical with and without the cache under Xvfb at 15 fps against a 1 MiB portrait (/tmp/pp-frames/big/); proved by the_first_frame_of_the_page_is_whole*
- **multi-pick-reports** A report can point at several things, says how they sit against each other, and Tab reaches what is behind *For you: Hold Shift as you click to point at more than one thing, and Tab or the wheel to pick something hidden behind what you can see; the report says which element contains, overlaps or sits apart from the other.* *No clip: a key-driven gesture inside a live editor; proved by a_person_reports_to_the_developers (shift_click_adds_a_second_pick_and_enter_finishes, tab_steps_down_the_stack_to_the_node_behind_the_pointer, a_plain_click_replaces_the_picks_it_finds) and a live Xvfb run that reads two picks and a relation out of the written record*
- **home-first-run** The Projects home says what DarkFactory is, how it works, and stops opening on a version receipt *For you: The first screen now tells you what DarkFactory is for, how the three steps work, and offers the tour — and it no longer opens with a note about which version you are running.* *No clip: copy and layout on one screen; proved by a_first_run_is_a_welcome_with_one_accent_button_that_opens_the_example, an_automatic_check_that_finds_nothing_raises_no_problem and an_up_to_date_check_says_the_version_is_the_newest*
- **update-answer** Check for updates says the answer instead of silently reverting to the same words *For you: Press Check for updates and it now tells you which version is the newest, instead of looking like nothing happened.* *No clip: one label and one line of text; proved by an_up_to_date_check_says_the_version_is_the_newest and the_up_to_date_answer_is_said_not_only_labelled*
- **feedback-reports** Two chords file a report on what is wrong: Cmd+D to the people who make DarkFactory, Cmd+Shift+D about your project *For you: Hold Cmd and press D, point at whatever is wrong, and say what it is; the people who build DarkFactory get the screen you saw. Cmd+Shift+D sends the same report about your own project to whoever is helping you build it.* *No clip: a key-driven gesture with no standing demo yet; proved by a_person_reports_to_the_developers and a_person_reports_about_this_project*

- **210-list-headings** `82049b0` The three list headings (PLACE, RIGS, PIECES) draw in the skin's ink, so a project's palette reaches the name of every list (#210) *No clip: invisible by design: the editor's own skin carries no label tone, so its look is unchanged; proved by list_headings_take_the_skins_ink.rs*
- **236-pose-inspector** `27d573e` The inspector edits the selection's pose through gooey's reflect inspector: a facing or lift change dispatches placement_set_pose or placement_set_lift, one undo step each (UI-50, #236) *No clip: an inspector's fields, not a new surface: the panel that shows them is the next slice (the multi-selection and its clip); proved by the_inspector_edits_through_the_operation.rs*
- **210-stage-chips** `49b619b` The rig bench's staging chips and their quiet line take the skin's ink, so a project's palette reaches the one row of the bench an author drives (#210) *No clip: invisible by design: the editor's own skin carries the same two colours, so its look is unchanged; proved by the_stage_chips_take_the_skins_ink.rs*
- **239-history-window** `09b80b4` The history panel reads only the rows it shows, so a long session's 5,000 entries cost seven labels a refresh instead of five thousand, and an agent's row says 'the NightShift' (UI-53, #239) *No clip: no visible change in a short session; proved by the_history_lists_only_the_rows_on_screen.rs (the window is the rows asked for, and agrees with the whole list)*
- **225-menu-bar** `88b8f45` The menu bar: every command in exactly one menu — File, Edit, View, Map, Meshes, Tiles, Compose, Rigs, Tools, Help — with the key it answers to, and a row that cannot run greys out and says why (UI-31, #225) *For you: A menu bar lists every command once, with its key, and greys out the ones that cannot run right now and says why.*<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-04-225-menu-bar.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-04-225-menu-bar.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>

- **283-scrollbar-wheel** `c5e98aa` A wheel over a panel's scrollbar scrolls the panel: the track forwards the notch to the area it belongs to (#283) *No clip: an input fix with no new surface; proved by a_wheel_over_the_inspector_scrolls_it::a_wheel_over_the_inspectors_scrollbar_scrolls_it (fails without the observer)*

- **269-note-pick** `0af1193` A Shift-click that picks no piece says so, naming the rectangle, instead of doing nothing and letting the next Enter send every draft (#269) *No clip: a one-line status change on a miss; proved by tests/enter_with_a_note_box_open_saves_it.rs::a_shift_click_that_picks_no_piece_says_what_to_do_instead*

- **trust-commands** `173abc6` A project's play.ron and agent.ron no longer start a program until a person allows it: Send and Play ask, with the exact command shown, and you can allow that command or trust the whole project *No clip: visible, not yet recorded: the question is the confirm dialog with a third button, and recording it needs the editor on a display; proved by crates/bevy_dark_factory/tests/a_project_command_needs_the_persons_trust.rs and crates/blueprint_model/tests/a_project_command_needs_the_persons_trust.rs*
- **268-warm** `9f6383b` The ground no longer vanishes for up to half a second after the first carve: the carved ground's pipelines are compiled before it is needed (#268)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-268-warm.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-268-warm.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **267-ground** `5a8d630` A flat ground is drawn clean: the bounds floor sits a centimetre under it, so the two are no longer one plane and the hatched pattern is gone (#267)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-267-ground.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-267-ground.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **101-ground-base** `fb7088f` The carved ground is an earth tone, not white, so the snow and wetness of volume_cover read against bare ground (#101)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-101-cover.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-101-cover.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **103-fire** `ae16a6b` A placement can carry a whole bevy_lights Fire: at 23:00 a torch and a brazier burn on two lamps, with their flames, sparks and pooled light, and the light map and light pool read their lumens (#103)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-103-fire.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-103-fire.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **266-triangle-count** `c4b103b` The editor no longer aborts when bevy_elements dresses the viewport: the triangle readout skips a mesh the render world took (#266) *No clip: a crash fix with no visible result of its own; proved by tests/a_mesh_the_render_world_took_does_not_crash_the_triangle_count.rs and a live run on big*
- **103-night-lights** `c4b103b` A night preview: with bevy_elements' sky the editor's own key light steps aside, so 23:00 on the Environment clock shows a dark room instead of a white sheet; two lit lamps and the light map show on it (#103)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-103-night-lights.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-103-night-lights.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **252-pack-switch** `cf582ef` The project configuration screen leaves a pack out of the kit, or brings it back: a switch per folder under assets/ is the pack_set_excluded operation Shift+R and an agent dispatch, project.ron keeps its prose, and a refusal is shown (#252)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-252-pack-switch.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-252-pack-switch.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **265-rebound** `b8fbd06` A rebound command answers to its new chord and no longer to the old one: the palette and the badges name it, and run_command presses it<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-265-rebound.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-265-rebound.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **252-family-toggle** `e54b9cd` The project configuration screen: a switch lists a family crate in family.ron, written atomically, and the screen shows the reload verdict<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-252-family-toggle.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-252-family-toggle.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **250-keymap** `75c9c74` The keymap screen: rebind a command's chord, and a chord another command has is refused with nothing saved<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-250-keymap-clash.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-250-keymap-clash.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **249-settings-screen** `dd0c703` The settings screen: Cmd+, opens every setting with its default, a search, tabs and a Reset that takes the key out of the file<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-249-settings-screen.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-249-settings-screen.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **233-refusal-toast** `5d85713` A refusal is a sticky bevy_gooey toast at the top of the viewport with Dismiss, and Show when it names a piece or a placement: Show selects it on the Meshes shelf (or targets it on the Map); the journal keeps the line (#233 slice 1)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-233-refusal-toast.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-233-refusal-toast.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>

## 2026-11-01

- **214-click-by-label** `6bc0cea` A UI test finds a widget by its label and clicks it with no window (the click used to be erased before picking read it) *No clip: a test-only change and a headless pointer fix; proved by tests/a_widget_is_clicked_by_its_label.rs*
- **221-loading** `4d0c23e` A project named on the command line opens on a loading screen as named stages: nothing for a second, then a spinner with the stage, then from ten seconds a bar with N of M; Cancel returns to the launcher with the project untouched (UI-20, #221)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-221-loading.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-221-loading.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **222-load-report** `5e8e0b3` After a project loads, a report card lists every file that does not read, one row each with the file, the field and why, with Open anyway and Back (UI-21, #222)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-221-loading.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-221-loading.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **216-launcher** `8ce3b16` A bare bevy_dark_factory opens the launcher: the recent projects as a gooey list with their folder and when they were opened, a typed folder, Open example, and the changelog since you last opened (UI-10, #216)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-216-launcher.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-216-launcher.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **218-new-project** `140260d` The launcher's New project wizard refuses an existing folder beside the Name field and keeps Create off with the reason; a free name writes assets/blueprint and opens it (UI-12, #218)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-216-launcher.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-216-launcher.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **220-whats-new** `8ce3b16` The launcher lists the CHANGELOG entries added since the last time a project was opened from it, and remembers the newest when one is opened (UI-14, #220)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-216-launcher.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-216-launcher.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **219-command-line** `8ce3b16` A bare bevy_dark_factory opens the launcher and a named project skips it; the launcher's Copy command line puts the line that opens the same project, kit and door on the clipboard and on screen (UI-13, #219) *No clip: a start-up rule and a clipboard line; proved by crates/bevy_dark_factory/tests/the_launcher_and_the_command_line_agree.rs*
- **263-game-data** `a0040e7` A running game follows its own data files: BlueprintLiveApp::reload_data_file takes an edit of a file data.ron lists about a second after it is written, and game_data leaves the restart list (#263) *No clip: a game-side API and a verdict, nothing new on screen; proved by crates/bevy_blueprint/tests/live_data.rs and a_game_data_file_that_does_not_read_is_named*
- **202-rigs-hot** `d9ee862` The animation tab takes an edit of rigs.ron at once: its rigs are measured again, the rig you were on stays selected by name, and rigs leaves the restart list (#202); the clip adds a rig to the manifest by hand and the new row appears in the rig list while the selection stays put<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-202-rigs-hot.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-202-rigs-hot.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **232-icon-atlas** `4834fe9` The editor has an icon atlas: 22 pixel-art glyphs, one for every toolbar command and dock panel, white on transparent so the skin tints them (UI-38, #232)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-232-icon-atlas.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-232-icon-atlas.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **129-kits** `a9bed25` An agent can make a kit (kit_create) and choose where new work lands (kit_set_authoring), and the project chooser's new-kit row and A are the same operations, each one log line with an undo that deletes nothing an author has added (#129 slice 20) *No clip: operations behind menu verbs that already existed, nothing new on screen; proved by kit_create_and_authoring_are_operations*
- **129-keep-set** `bb0af0f` M, a name and Enter keep the set in hand as a group through composition_define, which takes the set itself (set), so an agent defines a group from pieces that are not on the map and a person's group is one log line with one undo (#129 slice 14) *No clip: a key's routing and an operation parameter; proved by keeping_a_set_is_a_composition_operation*
- **129-tile-save** `da12b50` Cmd+S on the Tiles tab saves through composition_define, which takes a whole definition, so an agent writes a tile with slots, nested groups or an envelope of its own and a person's save is one log line with one undo (#129 slice 15) *No clip: a key's routing and an operation parameter; proved by saving_a_tile_is_a_composition_operation*
- **129-piece-fields** `da12b50` An agent can describe a library piece (piece_set_note) and resize it (piece_set_width), and a person's typed description, SIZE and wall height are the same operations, each one log line and one undo (#129 slices 16-18) *No clip: operations behind fields that already existed, nothing new on screen; proved by piece_note_and_width_are_operations and a_typed_wall_height_is_a_mount_operation*
- **129-mesh-scan** `fd7a197` B and the SCAN chip on the Meshes tab mark the cells a piece's mesh occupies through piece_set_cells' new scan verb, so an agent can scan a piece too: one log line, one undo (#129 slice 19) *No clip: a key's routing and an operation verb, nothing new on screen; proved by a_mesh_scan_is_a_cell_operation*
- **99-preset-field** `8d59af9` The Terrain field takes 'preset <name> <seed> <cell_m> <cells_x> <cells_z>', so a person bakes a bevy_elements terrain preset through the same operation an agent calls (#99)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-99-preset-field.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-99-preset-field.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **129-rig-adopt** `35dfc63` An agent can adopt a rig's measured gait values into rigs.ron (rig_adopt_measured), and Enter on the Anim tab is the same operation, one log line and one undo (#129 slice 13) *No clip: an operation; proved by tests/rig_adopt_measured_is_an_operation.rs*
- **129-accept-edges** `960f6a8` Enter on a mesh scan's derived edges keeps them through piece_set_cells, with a new per-cell edges verb an agent can call *No clip: a key's routing and an operation verb, nothing new on screen; proved by keeping_derived_edges_is_a_cell_operation*
- **262-bedroom-demos** `39f762a` A bedroom map ships in the repository's own project, and the eight standing demos and editor.png are re-recorded on it *No clip: the change is the standing demos themselves, in docs/media/; proved by tools/demo/stale.py (every row fresh) and the_shipped_bedroom_map_loads_against_the_shipped_kits*
- **oracle-rename** `cc40684` The decision-model crate is bevy_oracle (it was bevy_blueprint_decide): it asks typed questions and is not tied to one editor; the blueprint_decide MCP tool keeps its name *No clip: a crate rename; proved by the workspace gate*
- **dark_factory-help** bevy_dark_factory --help prints the arguments the editor reads and exits, instead of opening a window that names --help as a project *No clip: a command-line text; run: cargo run -q -p bevy_dark_factory -- --help*
- **pre-push-lfs** A push through the gate hook uploads the clips' LFS objects, so a clip's file reaches GitHub with its pointer and a push whose upload fails is refused (#149, #150) *No clip: the push gate itself; proved by tools/loop/test_pre_push_lfs.py (both tests fail with the previous hook)*
- **dark_factory-rename** `0e69943` The editor is the DarkFactory: crates/bevy_dark_factory and dark_factory_mcp, the window and the launcher say DarkFactory, and a blueprint is what it writes and a game loads (bevy_blueprint, blueprint_model and assets/blueprint/ keep their names) *No clip: a rename and a window title; proved by the workspace gate and cargo run -p bevy_dark_factory --example boot_headless*
- **129-vocab-add-token** `b57758f` An agent can add a token to the vocabulary: vocab_add_token, and Enter in the Shift+T prompt is the same operation, with the refusal shown in the prompt and the text kept (E-002, #129 slice 11) *No clip: an operation an agent sends and a prompt that did the same; proved by tests/vocab_add_token_is_an_operation.rs*
- **129-piece-set-cells** `e23a86e` An agent can mark, open, type and forget the cells of a library piece's lattice: piece_set_cells, and Z, X, V and the grid's chips and headers are the same operation with one undo (E-002, #129 slice 10) *No clip: an operation an agent sends and keys that did the same; proved by tests/piece_set_cells_is_an_operation.rs*
- **129-piece-turn** `1a9de8e` An agent can turn a library piece's mesh: piece_turn (id, axis, quarters, force) re-measures it in one act, and N, O, P, the rotate chips and the labeler's righting are the same operation with one undo (E-002, #129 slice 9) *No clip: an operation an agent sends and keys that did the same; proved by tests/piece_turn_is_an_operation.rs*
- **palette-rows-stand** `a47b7aa` A click on a palette row no longer respawns every row: the filter box was blurred on every click, the blur marked the filters changed, and the palette rebuilt, so a right-click's menu lost its row the next frame *No clip: a bug fix under a menu the next clip shows; proved by tests/a_palette_click_leaves_the_rows_standing.rs*
- **decide-game-114** `dd3eb14` blueprint_decide asks its checks and questions about the running game: a frame from its bevy_fairy and its state *No clip: an MCP tool's parameters, no screen of its own; proved by crates/bevy_blueprint_mcp/tests/decide_game.rs (a frame captured from fairy's demo game)*
- **ui-32-command-palette** `f67182d` Ctrl+Shift+P or Ctrl+K opens a command palette over every command: type undo, Edit: Undo is the first row with its shortcut, and Enter runs it as Ctrl+Z would (UI-32, #226)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-32-command-palette.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-32-command-palette.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **261-area-solve-just-the-area** `c42c260` A layout in an area solves just the area: two old pieces the rules would not set side by side no longer refuse the area between them, an enclosure wish counts the area's own cells, and a stamp of a symmetric tile at a turn the kit does not keep pins its edge (#261) *No clip: an operation parameter's solver; proved by a_solve_in_an_area_solves_just_the_area*
- **192-layout-area** `0958546` An agent can lay only part of a map: layout_propose takes an area, and keeping the layout replaces only the unpinned pieces inside it (graybox first, #192) *No clip: an operation parameter an agent sends, nothing new drawn; proved by bevy_blueprint_mapper/tests/a_layout_proposed_in_an_area_leaves_the_rest_of_the_map.rs*
- **185-balances** `be45309` A relation can keep a group balanced about a point: Balances(with, about, within) holds the mean of a piece and its group within so far of a point, relations_solve moves the piece to put the mean there, and the RELATIONS row reads it (research lens #2, #185) *No clip: an API and a RELATIONS row the agent reads, nothing drawn; proved by blueprint_model/tests/a_group_balanced_about_a_point.rs*
- **ui-36-piece-move-note** `9766629` Right-click a placed piece and choose Move or Note: Move picks that piece up and the next click puts it down, Note opens the note box on it and Enter leaves the note (UI-36, #230)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-36-piece-move-note.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-36-piece-move-note.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-36-palette-menu** `187e5ca` Right-click a piece's row in the palette and its menu opens: Place, Mount on floor, wall or ceiling, Remove from the kit, each the same act as its key or click and one line in the history (UI-36, #230)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-36-palette-menu.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-36-palette-menu.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-36-piece-menu** `7320751` Right-click a placed piece and its menu opens at the pointer: Turn left/right, Tip about x/z, Lift, Lower, Remove, each the same act as its key on that one piece and one line in the history; the clip removes a piece from the menu and Ctrl+Z puts it back (UI-36, #230)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-36-piece-menu.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-36-piece-menu.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **103-light-buttons** `e70a079` The light map and the pool budget are buttons: LIGHT MAP dispatches light_map and says its receipt, and POOL HERE dispatches light_pool at the camera's ground point; the clip shows two lit lamps lighting the map (#103)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-103-light-map-button.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-103-light-map-button.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-90-run-command** `b70184a` An agent runs any registered command by its id (blueprint_run_command), exactly as its shortcut would, and blueprint_state.ui says the screen, workspace, panels and focus (UI-90, #256) *No clip: an MCP tool; proved by a_command_runs_by_id_through_the_agents_door (4 tests)*
- **ui-07-writing-style** `335e231` Every command title follows the writing style: a test refuses abbreviations (verts, loc, rot, fac, obj, cfg, info), contractions and implementation terms, and the refused list is in docs/ui/vision.md §9 (UI-07, #215) *No clip: a test over the command titles; proved by every_label_follows_the_writing_style*
- **ui-04-command-registry** `4a4d6de` Every key is a command with a stable id, a title and a default shortcut (bevy_gooey Shortcut), and no two commands take one chord on a tab where both are live (UI-04, first slice; the menus and the palette dispatch by these ids) *No clip: an API the menus and the palette will read; proved by every_action_is_a_command (5 tests)*
- **103-flicker-preview** `8e958f2` A placed torch flickers in the viewport: a placement carrying a bevy_lights Flicker is drawn with a real light that the crate's own FlickerPlugin drives, retuned when the value changes and taken down when it is removed (#103)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-103-flicker-preview.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-02-103-flicker-preview.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **UI-40** `7bf3732` The editor's questions are gooey dialogs: confirm and the vocabulary prompt, with destructive buttons that name what goes<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-40-confirm.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-ui-40-confirm.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **ui-70-settings-layers** `9b2a24d` The editor's settings are three layers (defaults, <config_dir>/Blueprint/settings.ron, then the project's assets/blueprint/editor.ron), per key with the later layer winning, and a change to either file changes the running editor: deleting the project's key falls back to the user's value live, and a file that does not read keeps its last good content and is refused naming the field (UI-70) *No clip: no UI reads the settings yet (the settings screen is UI-71), so nothing is visible; proved by a_key_set_in_both_settings_layers_takes_the_projects*
- **fix-207-note-work-reads-the-log** `12d5956` A note's undo and an agent's fence refuse a log line that does not read, naming its line and field, instead of skipping it *No clip: a refusal in the operation log's readers; proved by note_work_reads_the_whole_log*
- **206-torn-oplog** `6867c9d` A write cut short loses one line of the operation log, not the next operation's too: a torn last line is ended before the next entry is written, so the entry is on its own line and reads *No clip: a crash-recovery fix in the log's writer; proved by a_torn_oplog_line_does_not_swallow_the_next_entry*
- **ui-02-editor-skin** `8579674` The editor's colours and measures are a bevy_gooey skin in src/editor_skin.ron (an eight-step elevation ladder that passes GooeySkin::problems()), and it is the running skin, so every gooey widget the editor adds draws in the editor's look (UI-02, first slice) *No clip: a data file and the running skin; the editor's chrome still draws from chrome.rs until the palette moves onto the skin, so nothing changes on screen; proved by editor_skin::the_editor_skin_breaks_none_of_the_librarys_own_rules*
- **ui-11-recent-projects** `b335efc` The editor remembers the projects it opened in <data_dir>/Blueprint/recent.ron, most recent first: opening one moves it to the top, a deleted folder is listed as missing rather than dropped, and an entry can be pinned, removed or located again (UI-11) *No clip: an API and a data file with no screen until the launcher (UI-10); proved by recent_projects::opening_a_project_moves_it_to_the_top_and_a_deleted_folder_is_listed_as_missing_not_dropped*
- **UI-05** `84857c2` A refused operation says the same what, where and fix on the toast, the journal row and an agent's answer (one `Problem` shape; `placement_remove` of an unknown id and an undo of a row that is gone name the entity and the fix) *No clip: an API and the text of a refusal; proved by tests/a_refused_operation_says_the_same_thing_on_every_surface.rs*
- **ui-01-gooey-required** `32b2aeb` The editor's UI library, bevy_gooey, is a required dependency in every build, and one EditorUiPlugin adds it (UI-01) *No clip: an API and a dependency change nobody can see; proved by editor_ui_plugin::a_default_build_has_the_gooey_skin_and_one_plugin_that_owns_it*
- **204-asset-spike** `a72c705` Bevy's asset system was tried on standards.ron beside the editor's own watchers, measured on Linux and macOS, and the watchers stay: every write was seen by both, in place and by rename, but a save of several files reaches the asset system spread over more than one frame (20 of 20 saves of three files 25 ms apart on Linux) where FileWatch says one reload is due after every save, a test of it needs the wall clock, and on macOS Bevy's watcher panics for a root that is not canonical (/var against /private/var); the spike stays behind the asset_spike cargo feature with its tests and its measuring example, and the guide has the numbers and when to read them again (#204) *No clip: a spike behind a cargo feature and a measurement, no visible change; proved by crates/bevy_blueprint_mapper/tests/asset_spike.rs (3 tests, run with --features asset_spike) and examples/asset_spike_measure.rs*
- **203-reload-state** `591955f` Every file of the open project now says what became of its latest edit, in a Reload panel in the inspector and in blueprint_state.reload_files: no game connected, game applied (with when), game refused (with the field and why), needs restart, or pending while the game has not answered an edit made after its last decision, with the editor's own verdict beside it; the editor also now reads a running game's verdict where the game writes it (the project directory, not the authoring kit's, so a real game's verdict never reached blueprint_state.game before) <br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-203-reload-state.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-203-reload-state.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **202-versions-4** `e3895e2` Every line of the operation log now carries a version, and a line of another version is refused by whoever names what is wrong: oplog.jsonl was the last format the editor writes with no version, an entry did not deny unknown fields so a line a newer build wrote was read as this build's; ops::Entry now has a root version (default 1 for a line written before it, so no log on disk is rewritten, and a refusal in its own deserialiser), every new line is written at version 1 and an append never rewrites a line, the reload verdict and the undo refuse a log holding such a line naming line N.version and version 2 is not the 1 this build reads and change nothing, and the readers that skip a line (the steps feed, the after-edit queue) skip it so the lines around it still show; the unversioned list is now empty and its ratchet 0, every kind of the contract table is versioned, versioned above core or owned elsewhere *No clip: a log format and its readers, nothing to see; proved by crates/bevy_blueprint_mapper/tests/an_oplog_line_of_another_version_is_refused.rs*
- **202-versions-3** `d56ce5d` A map's forest, people and triggers files and a checks.ron now carry a version, and every reader refuses one it does not read: a people or triggers file from a newer build was refused as damaged (Unexpected field named version) and a checks.ron of version 2 was taken as this build's because Checks never denied unknown fields; the refusal is now in each type's own deserialiser, naming the field version and the version this build reads (version 2 is not the 1 this build reads), so forest_scatter, person_set, trigger_set, the forest and checks views and Checks::for_project all refuse the same way and leave the bytes, a file from before the version reads as the first, and what the editor writes carries version 1; 28 kinds are versioned and only the operation log is not (the ratchet fell from 5 to 1) *No clip: a file format and its readers, nothing to see; proved by crates/bevy_blueprint_mapper/tests/a_forest_people_or_scripts_file_of_another_version_is_refused.rs and crates/bevy_blueprint_decide/tests/a_checks_file_of_another_version_is_refused.rs*
- **202-versions-2** `0743196` A vocabulary, the repetition rules, a terrain file, a water plan, a water field and a volume now carry a version, and every reader refuses one it does not read: a file from a newer build was read as this build's, and an editor operation that reads with a plain ron::from_str and writes the whole file back (terrain_stroke, water_place, volume_edit) wrote it back at this build's shape; the refusal is now in the type's own deserialiser, naming the version this build reads (version 2 is not the 1 this build reads), a file from before the version reads as the first so no project on disk is rewritten, and what the editor writes carries version 1; 24 kinds are versioned and 5 are still not (the ratchet fell from 11) *No clip: an API and a refusal, nothing to see; proved by crates/blueprint_model/tests/a_format_that_gains_a_version_reads_the_files_it_did_not_have_one_in.rs and crates/bevy_blueprint_mapper/tests/a_terrain_water_or_volume_file_of_another_version_is_refused.rs*
- **202-rebuild** `2935a4b` The reload contract lists what will always need a rebuild, with the reason for each, and audits every format for a version: a rules.ron or destructible.ron from a newer build was read as this build's (a reload said applied for a rules file the project open refuses, and destructible_set would have written the marks back at the older version) and is now refused naming the version the build reads; 18 kinds are versioned, 11 are still not (a ratchet that only shrinks) and 4 are another crate's to version *No clip: an API and a verdict, nothing to see; proved by crates/blueprint_model/tests/every_format_carries_a_version.rs and the_always_rebuild_list_is_in_the_guide.rs*
- **201-follow** `20670d4` What points at a piece follows it when a live reload draws the piece again: a person is not a placement so a reload never draws them and their position and ties survive any edit to a piece, but a person's home (a society's Home(entity)) or a list of bound places names a placement, and a placement an edit draws again has a new entity, so every such reference named an entity that was gone; now the game registers once, on the app, BlueprintLiveApp::follow_respawn::<C>(|c, respawned| c.0 = respawned.follow(c.0)) and after a reload that drew a piece again the closure runs once for every C in the world (the new entity for a piece drawn again, the same entity for a kept or removed one), a component marked changed only when something moved; a closure and not MapEntities because a game cannot implement that for another crate's component; bevy_blueprint 0.3.0 *No clip: an API with nothing to see; proved by crates/bevy_blueprint/tests/live_references.rs*
- **201-migrate** `0150191` A game's state follows a piece a live reload draws again, through one hook per kind of state: a moved, turned or retyped placement gets a new entity and the old one is despawned, so a booking, an animation phase or a camera target on it was lost and every call site that edits a piece had to patch it; now the game registers once, on the app, BlueprintLiveApp::carry_across_respawn::<C>() (a component copied as it is) or migrate_on_respawn(|world, old, new| ...) (anything else), and the hook runs once for each piece drawn again (a rider drawn again with its host too, a piece renamed and drawn again under its new id) after the new entity is drawn and before the old one is despawned, and never for a piece the edit left alone or removed (bevy_blueprint 0.2.1; the in-place move, where a moved piece keeps its entity, needs the owner to change the assertion in live_reload.rs that pins a respawn) *No clip: an API for a game's own state, nothing to watch in the editor; proved by crates/bevy_blueprint/tests/live_migrate.rs (6 tests, the file did not compile before)*
- **201-rename** `d0c9f1a` A placement renamed in the project's files keeps its entity, and what a game hung on it, across a live reload: the game's BlueprintLivePlugin used to read a renamed placement as one removal and one addition, so a booking, an animation phase or a camera target on its entity was lost though the piece had not changed; now a placement whose id changed and whose row did not is a rename (MapDiff::renamed from the new diff_maps_renaming, found by content, paired only when unambiguous, riders on a renamed host and a stamp whose id changed follow), its Entity stays with the new id in its Placement, and a reload that writes the same content draws nothing *No clip: an API and a reload behaviour with no picture of its own (an entity id that does not change); proved by crates/bevy_blueprint/tests/live_identity.rs and crates/blueprint_model/tests/live_diff_renames.rs*
- **200-reference-family** `82f3e34` A referenced picture that is not a picture, and a family settings file that is not the crate's own type, are named instead of passing: a reference whose file a full disk cut off, a text saved over or another format renamed used to look fine until an agent opened it, and an elements or gooey settings file with a typo'd field was reported applied while the crate threw it away; now blueprint_state.reloads names the file and the field (kind reference_images: signature, format, header, width, height; kind family: clock, climate, accent), once per edit, the last good settings stay, and every kind in the registry has a reader that names its file and field (#200, H3) *No clip: a reload verdict and a status line, nothing to see; proved by core tests/a_reference_image_names_its_field.rs (5 tests), the editor's a_reference_image_that_does_not_read_is_named (4) and, with --features bevy_elements,bevy_gooey, family_settings_typed_verdicts (3)*
- **200-destructible** `d47626b` A destructible piece's damaged marks file or bake is named instead of overwritten: a hand edit that cut destructible.ron off, turned its version into a word or dropped pieces used to reach destructible_set as the parser's position with no field, and a bake that did not parse was baked again over the damaged bytes with no word; now reload_check reads both kinds in every build (the schemas moved into core), and destructible_set and destructible_hit refuse naming the file and the field (destructible.ron (pieces), destructible/site__cart.bake.ron (chunks[1].points)), write nothing, and leave the file as it is, while a bake made from another mesh is still baked again *No clip: a refusal's wording on an operation that reads a file, nothing to see; proved by core tests/reload_verdicts.rs (two more kinds, three bad files each), tests/a_destructible_file_names_its_field.rs, tests/old_data.rs (two frozen real files) and mapper tests/a_destructible_file_that_does_not_read_is_named.rs (4 tests, with the bevy_carnage feature)*
- **200-game-data** `ace7b65` A game's data file that no longer reads is named instead of silently dropping its rows: a hand edit that cut a tuning file off, turned a bounded leaf into a word or removed it used to leave the Data panel empty and data_set refusing without a file, and now blueprint_state.reloads names the file and the leaf (kind game_data, `mob.speed`, one verdict per edit, the reason says what a registered file is missing or holds), says needs_restart for an edit that reads (a running game uses it only if it re-reads its data) and applied when the file is back to the text it first had, reports the registry itself as data_registry, and data_set over a file that does not scan refuses naming the file and the leaf and leaves its bytes *No clip: a reload verdict and a refusal's wording, nothing to see; proved by tests/a_game_data_file_that_does_not_read_is_named.rs (6 tests) and core tests/a_data_file_names_its_leaf_and_field.rs (8 tests)*

## 2026-10-31

- **200-oplog-lines** `47777d7` A damaged line in oplog.jsonl is named instead of skipped without a word: a hand edit or a write cut short used to cost the author what that line recorded and say nothing, and now blueprint_state.reloads names the file, the line and the field of the first line that does not read (kind oplog, `line 3.seq`, `line 3.op` for a missing field, `line 3` for text that is not JSON, one verdict per edit, none for an append to a log that reads), the readers that skip still skip, and an undo past the line refuses naming it and writes nothing *No clip: a reload verdict and a refusal's wording, nothing to see; proved by tests/a_damaged_oplog_line_is_named_and_the_rest_still_reads.rs (4 tests) and core tests/a_json_lines_file_names_its_damaged_line_and_field.rs (7 tests)*
- **200-checks-view** `dc12659` A bad hand edit to checks.ron no longer stops every after-edit check: the editor holds the last good checks in memory, so a typo, a wrong type, a missing field or a threshold outside 0..=1 leaves the checks running as they were and blueprint_state.reloads names the file and the field (kind checks, one verdict per edit); a file that is there and cannot be read is refused, not treated as removed *No clip: a reload verdict and a held value, nothing to see; proved by tests/a_refused_checks_file_keeps_the_last_good_checks.rs (6 tests)*
- **200-old-data** `40b9698` An edit to a settings type that breaks the files already on disk is refused, naming the field: tests/old_data freezes one file of every kind check reads as a release wrote it (the first release's project files byte for byte, 0.3.0's for the rest) and reads each with the type's own reader, so a field added with no default, or removed while old files still carry it, fails naming the kind, the file, the field and the release; with a required field added to Standards the three-bad-files tests still passed and this one failed (#200 step 5, H3) *No clip: a schema-evolution check on settings types, nothing to film; proved by crates/blueprint_model/tests/old_data.rs (3 tests)*
- **200-needs-restart** `168a471` An edit to rigs.ron now says what became of it: the animation tab reads the manifest when it loads and holds it, so a hand edit used to do nothing and say nothing, and a broken one looked the same as a good one; blueprint_state.reloads now says needs_restart for a file that reads (the third outcome beside applied and refused: it is on disk, the tab keeps what it holds, change door and come back or restart), refused with the file and the field for one that does not read, once per edit however many frames pass, and the verdict clears when the file is put back or the door changes *No clip: an editor reload verdict, said in blueprint_state.reloads and once on the status line; nothing to film beyond a line of text; proved by mapper tests/a_rigs_edit_says_what_became_of_it.rs (5 tests) and core tests/a_restart_verdict_says_so.rs*
- **200-notes-and-family** `20872c1` A bad hand edit to a map's notes file no longer empties the signs and the Notes panel, and notes and family settings say what became of an edit: the editor holds the last good notes in memory, so a typo, a wrong type or a missing field leaves every sign and row as it was, and blueprint_state.reloads names the file and the field (kind notes, one verdict per edit); an edit to a family settings file is a verdict too (kind family: applied, refused with why while the last good settings stay, or gone) (#200 slice 5, H3) *No clip: an editor reload behaviour, a refused file leaves what is shown as it was; proved by tests/a_refused_notes_file_keeps_the_last_good_notes.rs and tests/family_settings_verdicts.rs*
- **200-tree-files** `0b3a0c9` A bad hand edit to a tree file no longer deletes the trees drawn from it: the forest view keeps the last good value of every .tree.ron in memory, so a typo, a wrong type or a field the crate does not know leaves the trees standing exactly as they were and the editor says which file and field in blueprint_state.reloads (kind trees, one verdict per edit), tree_edit refuses to write over a tree file it cannot read and names the file and the field, and an unknown field is named once (undergrow, not undergrow.undergrow); the unreadable kinds left on the editor's ratchet drop from 8 to 7 (#200 slice 4, H3)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-200-tree-file-bad-edit.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-200-tree-file-bad-edit.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **200-unread-files** `c05f917` A forest, people or triggers file that does not read is never overwritten: forest_scatter, person_set and trigger_set read the map's file, change one row and wrote all of it back, and a file with a typo counted as no file, so one hand-edit mistake lost every other row; now the call is refused naming the file and the field and the bytes stay as they were, people_run says the same instead of 'nobody lives here', and the forest view decides its file once per edit into blueprint_state.reloads and keeps the trees drawn (#200 slice 3, H3) *No clip: an operation refuses a file it cannot read and leaves it as it was, and a view reports a verdict; nothing to film; proved by mapper tests/a_file_that_does_not_read_is_never_overwritten.rs*
- **loop-ship-audit** every loop pass is audited for what it shipped: commits on main, unpushed work, a release when the package changed, docs and demos, with what was missing, on each repo's loop-stats branch; the loops release every pass that changed the package *No clip: loop tooling and commands; proved by tools/loop/test_steps.py (8 tests) and the driver's test scripts*
- **200-editor-verdicts** `8f03593` A bad hand edit to a terrain, water or volume file no longer changes what the editor draws, and the editor says which file and field: the ground, the water and the carved volume each keep their last good value in memory, so water that does not fill its shape, a carve with no size or a field of the wrong type is refused with its field named, said once on the status line and in blueprint_state.reloads (applied or refused, the field, the reason, one number per edit), and a deleted file is no longer refused; the water plan, water field, volume and data registry join the kinds check can read, 15 unreadable kinds down to 11 (#200 slice 2, H3)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-200-terrain-bad-edit.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-200-terrain-bad-edit.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **loop-step-times** every step of a loop pass is timed: tools/loop/steps.py reads the pass's omp session into ~/.local/state/wiggum/<clone>/steps.tsv after each pass, a pass marks its phases, and steps.py report ranks where a loop's time goes *No clip: loop tooling, nothing in the editor changes; proved by tools/loop/test_steps.py (6 tests) and the driver's own four test scripts*
- **129-piece-remove** `237e9ac` Delete on a library piece sends it back out of the library through piece_remove, the operation an agent calls: an agent can take a piece out (refused while a map places it or a composition holds it, nothing written), Delete writes one log line, one Cmd+Z puts the whole piece back where it stood and Cmd+Shift+Z sends it out again, and the shelf follows either front end (#129 slice 8) *No clip: an operation behind a key that already existed; proved by tests/piece_remove_is_an_operation.rs (an agent's removal and undo, refusals write nothing, a preview, a person's Delete, Cmd+Z and redo) and operations_have_two_front_ends::*
- **129-pack-set-excluded** `9e310f2` Shift+R leaves a pack out of the kit, or brings it back, through pack_set_excluded, the operation an agent calls: an agent can say a folder of art is not what the kit is built from, a bad or missing folder is refused with nothing written, the key writes one log line and one Cmd+Z takes the newest press back (even after the folder is gone), and a bare undo no longer risks taking it for the map's (#129 slice 7) *No clip: an operation behind a key that already existed; proved by tests/pack_set_excluded_is_an_operation.rs (an agent's exclusion and undo, refusals write nothing, a restore undone after its folder is deleted, a bare undo, a person's Shift+R and Cmd+Z) and operations_have_two_front_ends::*
- **129-piece-set-mount** `7898146` M sets a library piece's mount through piece_set_mount, the operation an agent calls: an agent can say where a piece goes, M steps past rungs the project would refuse, one log line and one Cmd+Z per press, and a bare undo no longer takes a library change for the map's (#129 slice 6) *No clip: an operation behind a key that already existed; proved by tests/piece_set_mount_is_an_operation.rs (an agent's mount and undo, refusals write nothing, a person's M and Cmd+Z, a bare undo) and operations_have_two_front_ends::*
- **200-per-file-verdicts** `5832f5b` A running game that refuses a reload says which file and which field: the verdict it writes (.live/game.ron, version 2) has one applied or refused entry for every file the load reads, with the kind, the path, the field (version, placements[3].at[1]) and the reason, a sequence number so one edit is one verdict however many frames pass, and a refused edit still changes no entity and no world; blueprint_state.game and the journal carry it (#200 slice 1, H3) *No clip: an API and a file format an agent reads, nothing a person sees; proved by core tests/reload_verdicts.rs (three bad files for each of 18 kinds), bevy_blueprint tests/live_verdicts.rs (the game keeps its entities and world, one verdict per edit) and the mapper's the_games_per_file_verdicts_reach_blueprint_state*
- **199-atomic-writes** `2d7fdf7` Every write of a project file is atomic: a reader (the running game, the editor's watcher) sees the old file or the new one, never half of either. One write path, blueprint_model::atomic_file, writes a uniquely named hidden temp file beside the target, flushes it, renames it over the target and flushes the directory; 19 plain writes (terrain, water, volume, forest, trees, people, scripts, family, game data, destructible, reference images, kits.ron and vocab.ron at creation, and two caches) moved onto it, and a ratchet test fails on a new one (#199) *No clip: an I/O guarantee with nothing to see; proved by core tests/atomic_writes.rs (a reader thread over 10,000 replacements, a child process killed at 40 random points, four writers of one path) and tests/atomic_writes_only.rs*
- **198-reload-contract** `19bd4d2` The reload contract: every kind of file the editor writes (33 kinds) has a reload class (Live, Respawn or Restart) and a lifetime (global or per map) in blueprint_model::reload, each operation declares the kinds it writes, and docs/guide/reload-contract.md is the audit table rendered from them; a kind with no class fails every_written_kind_has_a_reload_class and a kind that joins Restart off the checked-in list fails the_restart_list_only_shrinks (#198, H1) *No clip: an audit table and two ratchet tests, no editor surface; proved by crates/blueprint_model/tests/reload_contract.rs (3 tests) and crates/bevy_blueprint_mapper/tests/reload_contract.rs (4 tests), each ratchet shown failing on a mutated list*
- **193-viewport-under-sky** `ed2bec0` With a family/bevy_elements.ron the editor's viewport draws the map's box and grid under the elements sky instead of one flat colour: the interface camera no longer copies the ground colour back over the world (#193, #194)<br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-10-31-193-world-under-sky.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-10-31-193-world-under-sky.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>
- **129-placement-set-pose** `0f79607` V straightens a placed piece through placement_set_pose, the operation an agent calls: an agent can set a piece's turn and tip in one change, V writes one log line and one Cmd+Z takes both back (#129 slice 5) *No clip: an operation behind a key that already existed; proved by tests/placement_set_pose_is_an_operation.rs (an agent's pose and undo, refusals write nothing, a person's R, Y, V and Cmd+Z) and operations_have_two_front_ends::*
- **196-f32-undo** `ad69737` Undo no longer refuses a turn to 3.9 degrees (or any f32 the log's text spelled one step off) as an edit outside the log: the log is read back with serde_json's exact float parser (#196) *No clip: a fix in how the operation log's numbers are read; proved by tests/an_f32_in_the_log_still_undoes.rs (a turn to five yaws and a settings change, failing before)*
- **coven-docs** the README follows the bevy-coven documentation guide: the Vibe Coded banner, badges, Status and Why lines, a still beside the GIF, the NightShift named, and the 64-second demo moved to the book page *No clip: a README and docs change; the README's GIFs are unchanged and the demo video now plays on bevy-coven.github.io/crates/bevy_blueprint.html; proved by every image URL answering 200*
- **blueprint-solver-0.1.1** blueprint_solver 0.1.1 on crates.io: its docs.rs page shows the N-queens GIF, now an absolute URL *No clip: a release whose only change is the README image URL; proved by the docs.rs page's image answering 200*
- **blueprint-rename** `8ade561` the editor is Blueprint: crates bevy_blueprint_*, MCP tools blueprint_*, env vars BLUEPRINT_*, and the project data folder assets/blueprint; a project still in assets/emerge is moved once when the editor opens it, and refused when both folders exist *No clip: a rename; the only new pixels are the word Blueprint in the window title and the menu's chrome bar; proved by blueprint_model/tests/a_project_in_assets_emerge_is_moved_once.rs*
- **129-placement-set-lift** `8455e35` [ and ] lift a placed piece through placement_set_lift, the operation an agent calls: an agent can raise a lamp off its table, the pieces resting on it ride with it, and it is undone like any other (#129 slice 4) *No clip: an operation behind two existing keys, nothing new to see; proved by tests/placement_set_lift_is_an_operation.rs*
- **179-busy-poll** `9b10173` emerge_capture_sequence keeps waiting when the busy editor misses one status poll's 5 s budget, instead of abandoning the clip (#179) *No clip: a fix to the MCP server's capture-sequence poll; proved by bevy_emerge_mcp/tests/a_slow_status_poll.rs*
- **E-006-publish-bevy_emerge_anim** `dd1fc52` bevy_emerge_anim 0.1.0, which blends clip weights on a skinned rig's animation player, is on crates.io: with it every crate a game links is on the registry (#128) *No clip: a release to crates.io, nothing to see in the editor; proved by the crates.io index entry for bevy_emerge_anim 0.1.0 (pubtime 2026-10-31T01:57:45Z) and the bevy_emerge_anim-v0.1.0 tag*
- **129-placement-set-tip** `aab1f59` Y and U tip a placed piece through placement_set_tip, the operation an agent calls: an agent can lay a piece on its side, and it is refused for the same reasons a person is (a host, an unmeasured piece) (#129, slice 3) *No clip: an operation behind keys that already existed; proved by placement_set_tip_is_an_operation:: (agent tip and undo, refusals write nothing, Y is one log line and one Cmd+Z) and operations_have_two_front_ends::*

- **188-relations-end-stack** `7a29aee` A relation's end is its whole stack: a jug on the counter no longer blocks the counter's ClearInFront, nor the stall a sign hangs on its VisibleFrom (#188) *No clip: a fix to two relation checks in core; proved by crates/bevy_emerge_core/tests/a_relations_end_is_its_whole_stack.rs*
- **192-solve-in-area** `3b4598a` A generate can be asked to lay only inside an area: core grammar::solve_in and solve_constrained_in fill the area's cells, join the pieces around it, and leave the rest of the map as it is *No clip: an API (core grammar::Area, solve_in, solve_constrained_in), not yet reachable from the editor (slice 2); proved by crates/bevy_emerge_core/tests/a_generate_lays_only_inside_its_area.rs*
- **E-006-publish-bevy_emerge** `2633021` bevy_emerge 0.1.0, the runtime crate that spawns a map in a Bevy world, is on crates.io: a game adds it with cargo add bevy_emerge instead of a path (#128) *No clip: a release to crates.io, nothing to see in the editor; proved by the crates.io index entry for bevy_emerge 0.1.0 (created 2026-10-31T01:11Z) and the bevy_emerge-v0.1.0 tag*
- **B-187-terrain-shape** `d2d9762` A terrain file whose heights do not fill its shape (a hand edit, a truncated write) no longer panics the editor: it draws no ground, names terrain/<map>.ron on the status line, and terrain_stroke and water_run refuse it (#187) *No clip: a bug fix: what it removes is a panic; proved by bevy_emerge_core/tests/a_terrain_file_that_does_not_fill_its_shape_is_refused.rs and the editor's a_terrain_file_that_does_not_fill_its_shape_is_refused::*
- **186-long-pieces-half-turn** `bcb413e` repetition_vary turns a long clutter piece (a bench, a cart) only a half turn, so it never lands on cells it did not cover *No clip: a fix to repetition_vary's plan (which copies turn, and by how much); proved by crates/bevy_emerge_core/tests/vary_never_turns_a_long_piece_off_its_cells.rs*
- **103-overlay** `1768ce8` The light map is drawn over the open map: a LIGHT MAP button shows bevy_light_grid's field, blue where dark and amber where lit, and it follows the map as torches are lit (#103) <br><a href="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-103-light-map-button.mp4"><img src="docs/changelog/media/2026-11-01-103-light-map-button.png" width="320" alt="clip"></a>

- **180-pointer-read-first** `c488d07` A click lands where the cursor is this frame: the pointer is read before every Update system, so the Note tool's second Shift-click no longer takes the first piece back out (#180) *No clip: an input-ordering fix with nothing new on screen; proved by the_pointer_is_read_before_anything_acts_on_it and 51 of 51 batch runs of a_person_shift_clicks_two_pieces_into_one_note*

## 2026-10-27

- **Behaviour change (`089705f`, #82): a guest `on` a host lands on the host's lid.** `stack::surface_lift` now takes away the `align.y_offset` that `datum` already added, so the lid stands `extent.height` above the host's *base*. Hosts with no `y_offset` are unchanged. A consumer that compensated for the old arithmetic (declaring `ymax`, not `ymax − ymin`) now sinks guests into the lid by the host's `y_offset`. **Migration: declare `ymax − ymin`, not `ymax`.** *No clip: not visible: a placement rule, its documentation and a core API; proved by the tests in `089705f`*
- `Extent::height` is documented as base-to-top, independent of `y_offset` (#83). *No clip: not visible: a placement rule, its documentation and a core API; proved by the tests in `089705f`*
- `Library::height_disagreements(asset_root, tolerance_m)` compares every surface-offering descriptor's declared height with its glb and lists the ones that disagree, plus those it could not measure (#83). *No clip: not visible: a placement rule, its documentation and a core API; proved by the tests in `089705f`*
- **E-041** `203fbc3` A test can click a palette row *No clip: not visible: test harness; proved by the tests in its commit*
